L359: Treatment-Naïve and PRS-Experienced GT2 Subjects Without Cirrhosis
L360: Genotype 2 TN and TE-PRS subjects were enrolled predominately in ENDURANCE-2 and
L361: Clinical ReviewLarissa Stabinski, MDAimee Hodowanec, MDNDA 209394Mavyret® (glecaprevir and pibrentasvir)
L362: SURVEYOR-2. Approximately 43% (202/466) of the overall G2 subjects were enrolled in ENDURANCE-2, which was a Phase 3, randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled (delayed treatment) multicenter study to evaluate the safety and efficacy of GLE/PIB for 12 weeks in HCV GT2-infected, non-cirrhotic (NC) subjects, (either TN or TE-PRS). Among this sample of HCV GT2-infected, non-cirrhotic (NC) subjects, the SVR12 was 99.5% with a 95% CI of (97.2, 100), with only one subject failing to achieve SVR12 due to missing the post-treatment 12 week assessment; there were no virologic failures. The SVR12 results among GT2-infected subjects treated with 12 weeks of GLE/PIB was 100%, providing strong support for a 12 week duration, but not the requested 8 week duration.
L363: Support for the 8-week duration in GT-2 non-cirrhotic subjects comes from SURVEYOR-2 Part 4 and Part 2. SURVEYOR-2 was a multi-arm, multi-part, open label Phase 2 trial that evaluated the safety and efficacy of GLE/PIB, co-administered or combined, with or without ribavirin (RBV) in adults with HCV GT 2, 3, 4, 5, or 6 infection who are TN or TE-PRS with or without cirrhosis for 8 and 12 weeks duration.
L364: Within SURVEYOR-2, 143 TN or PRS-TE non-cirrhotic GT2 subjects were enrolled in Part 4, and 54 TN or PRS-TE non-cirrhotic GT2 subjects were enrolled in Part 2; all 197 subjects received GLE/PIB for 8 weeks.
L365: The SVR12 rate from SURVEYOR-2 for combined Parts 2 and 4, in TN and TE-PRS subjects without cirrhosis was 97.9% (193/197) with a 95%CI of (94.5, 99.2) ; 2 subjects experienced virologic failure. The efficacy determination for SURVEYOR-2 Part 4 alone, was a comparison to the external threshold SVR12 rate of 89%. This historical threshold is considered clinically relevant as it was based on the SVR12 rate from the currently approved standard-of-care, SOF+ RBV for 12 weeks, at the time of the trial initiation. The SVR rate was 98.5% (135/137) with a Wilson method 95% CI of (94.8%, 99.6%); with the lower bound of the 95%CI well above the 89% threshold.
L366: TN and PRS-Experienced GT2 Subjects With Compensated Cirrhosis
L367: Most [31/35] 88.6% GT2 subjects with compensated cirrhosis were enrolled in EXPEDITION-1. This was a single-arm, open-label, multicenter study to evaluate the safety and efficacy of a 12-week regimen of GLE/PIB for treating GT 1, 2, 4, 5 or 6 HCV-infected adult subjects with compensated cirrhosis who are either TN or TE-PRS. Although the Applicant claimed this trial was a Phase 3 trial, this open label single arm trial lacked a formal hypothesis, and hence was evaluated as a Phase 2 study by the FDA statistics team. The SVR12 rate was 100% (31/31), and there were no GT-2 virologic failures in this study. There were no major additional statistics considerations. Please see the statistics review for further details.
L368: Clinical ReviewLarissa Stabinski, MDAimee Hodowanec, MDNDA 209394Mavyret® (glecaprevir and pibrentasvir)
L369: The remaining 4 GT2 cirrhotic subjects were enrolled in EXPEDITION-4, and received 12 weeks of GLE/PIB. All 4 subjects achieved SVR12.
L370: Clinical Virology Considerations:
L371: Within the population of HCV GT2 infected subjects without cirrhosis, virologic failure occurred in 2/197 (1%) subjects treated with GLE/PIB for 8 weeks. The two failures were due to relapse and occurred in treatment-experienced subjects. One of these subjects had low GLE exposure possibly related to a medical history of gastric bypass.
L372: As in HCV GT1 infected subjects, the near 100% efficacy of GLE/PIB for subjects with HCV GT2 infection indicates that natural baseline resistance-associated polymorphisms did not significantly affect treatment outcome. Several HCV GT2 subtypes were represented, with subtypes 2a, 2b and 2c being most common. Both virologic failure subjects had a subtype 2a infection with an NS5A M31 sequence, which is the consensus sequence for this subtype. Neither subject had treatment-emergent or treatment-enriched substitutions at known resistance-associated positions at the time of failure.
L373: The 1% (2/197) relapse rate with no evidence of baseline or treatment-emergent resistance in the 2 subjects that relapsed support recommending GLE/PIB for 8 weeks for noncirrhotic, HCV GT2 infected patients. Furthermore, all 35 cirrhotic HCV GT2 infected subjects treated with GLE/PIB for 12 weeks achieved SVR12.
L374: Additional Clinical Data/ Considerations: Additional details regarding the two subjects who experienced relapse are briefly described below. Although data were limited given that there were only two subjects with virologic failure, there was no indication that age, sex or race played a role in these virologic failures.
L375: Subject 2917 is a 56 year old white female with a relevant history of gastric bypass, without cirrhosis who experienced a relapse at post treatment week 12. She had a history of prior treatment with SOF and RBV. The subject experienced no AEs on treatment but was found to have exposures of GLE on Day 1 and Week 4 that were &gt; 75% lower than other subjects in the same treatment arm. The exposures of PIB in this subject were comparable to other subjects in the cohort .
L376: Subject 7016 is a 55 year old non-cirrhotic white male with prior P/R treatment experience who experienced relapse at post treatment week 12. The subject experienced no adverse events and had normal drug levels.
L377: Reviewer Comment: Given the high SVR rates and robust data for GT2 TN and TE-PRS subjects in this application, the review team supports the indication of 8 weeks of therapy for
L378: subjects without cirrhosis and 12 weeks of therapy for subjects with compensated cirrhosis (CTP A liver disease) in this population. As was noted for GT1 subjects, from a general safety standpoint, this indication also minimizes the exposure time to GLE/PIB, decreasing the possibility of any associated adverse events and possibility for drug-drug interactions without sacrificing efficacy.
L379: GENOTYPE 3
L380: Overall Genotype 3(GT3) Data:
L381: As shown in Table 8, there were a total of 643 HCV GT3, TN and PRS-TE subjects who were treated with GLE/PIB in the Phase 2/3 dataset. Of these, 116 (18.0%) were subjects with compensated cirrhosis and 521 (81.0%) were treatment naïve. Subjects with treatment experience either had INF or P/R experience or experience with SOF and RBV or P/R. All were treated with GLE/PIB with or without RBV for 8, 12 or 16 weeks duration. Overall, three non-cirrhotic subjects and one cirrhotic subject experienced on-treatment virologic failure and 14 non-cirrhotic subjects and 2 cirrhotic subjects experienced relapse. Fifteen subjects did not achieve SVR12 for non-virologic reasons, eight were missing SVR12 data and seven discontinued treatment. For the statistical evaluations, only treatment regimens without RBV were considered.

L388: ENDURANCE-3- Treatment Naïve without cirrhosis
L389: The majority, (74%, 390/527) of non-cirrhotic GT 3 subjects in the Phase 2/3 efficacy dataset were enrolled in ENDURANCE-3. This was a Phase 3, randomized, open-label, active-controlled (SOF/DCV), multicenter, non-inferiority trial to assess the efficacy and safety of GLE/PIB in GT3-infected, TN subjects without cirrhosis.
L390: This trial included three treatment arms, GLE/PIB for 12 weeks (arm A, N=233), SOF/DCV for 12 weeks (arm B, N=116) and GLE/PIB for 8 weeks (arm C, N=157). Randomization was conducted in a 2:1 ratio for arm A: arm B; once enrollment to arms A and B were completed, subjects were assigned to arm C in a non-randomized manner. Arm C was added to the trial design following review of Phase 2 data, input from the Division, and after the trial was initiated. The trial objectives were first to test for non-inferiority of GLE/PIB for 12 weeks versus SOF/DCV based on a 6% margin, followed by a test for superiority of the 12 week arm to the active control.
L391: The Applicant's algorithm for evaluating efficacy of the GLE/PIB 8w regimen involved assessing the lower bound of the CI around the 8w SVR12 and also of the CI around the difference (8w-12w). Since the lower bound the CI around the point estimate of the 8w SVR12 was 91.5% (applicant's analysis), the applicant calculated the adjusted 97.5% CI (-5.4, 4.6) around the difference (-0.4%) between GLE/PIB groups.
L392: The FDA statistical reviewer's assessment of the 8w arms' efficacy differed from the applicant's. Specifically, the reviewer focused on the lower bound of the confidence interval around the SVR12 of 90.3% (Wilson method), which is slightly below than the target historical SVR12 rate of 92%. Secondly, the reviewer's analysis of the difference (8W-SOF/DCV) in SVR12 generated a difference of -1.6% and corresponding 97.5% confidence interval (adjusted for multiple testing) of -7.7% to 5.1%. These results do not meet the -6%NI margin; however, these findings suggest that the 8w regimen confers a clinically acceptable SVR12 rate ranging from 90% to 97% and as much as 8% worse than the active-comparator, SOF/DCV, but fails to demonstrate superiority over the historical threshold of 92%.

L403: Clinical Virology Considerations:
L404: Pooled SVR12 rates for HCV GT3 infected subjects ranged from approximately 90 to 100% across various GLE/PIB durations and patient populations. For treatment-naïve, noncirrhotic HCV GT3 infected subjects, pooled SVR12 rates (non-VF-censored) were 97% (177/183) and 99% (258/261) for those who received GLE/PIB for 8 or 12 weeks, respectively, with a 1.9% higher relapse rate [(n=5) 2.7% vs. (n=3) 0.8%] with the 8-week versus 12-week duration. No treatment-naïve, HCV GT3 infected subjects with cirrhosis who received GLE/PIB for 12 weeks experienced virologic failure (n=64, non-VF-censored).
L405: For treatment-experienced (IFN- or SOF-based), HCV GT3 infected subjects without cirrhosis, relapse rates were 8.3% (4/48) and 4.5% (1/22) with 12- and 16-week GLE/PIB durations, respectively. Similarly, a 4% (2/50) relapse rate was observed for treatment-experienced, HCV GT3 infected subjects with cirrhosis who received GLE/PIB for 16 weeks. Therefore, from a virologic standpoint a conservative 16-week GLE/PIB duration was recommended for treatment-experienced (IFN- or SOF-based), HCV GT3 infected subjects with or without cirrhosis in order to reduce the risk of relapse.
L406: Across all GLE/PIB treatment regimens, 98% of HCV GT3 infected subjects had a subtype 3a infection. There was not a clear association between the detection of NS3 resistance-associated polymorphisms and treatment outcome with GLE/PIB for subjects with GT3 infection. However, it was found that baseline and treatment-emergent resistance data
L407: indicate that certain NS5A resistance-associated polymorphisms, particularly A30K, may influence GLE/PIB efficacy for GT3. The A30K polymorphism was detected at a ≥15% nucleotide sequence level in 6% of GT3a baseline sequences.
L408: Among treatment-naïve, noncirrhotic subjects treated with GLE/PIB for 8 or 12 weeks, 9 subjects experienced virologic failure (excluding 1 suspected reinfection), of whom 5 (56%) had subtype 3a virus with an NS5A A30K polymorphism. Relapse rates for GT3a subjects with the A30K polymorphism were 17.6% (3/17) and 0% (0/13) for subjects who received the 8-week and 12-week durations, respectively. The A30K or Y93H polymorphisms were also enriched among treatment-experienced, HCV GT3 infected subjects who experienced virologic failure with GLE/PIB for 12 or 16 weeks, although insufficient data were available to assess the impact of these polymorphisms specifically on the efficacy of the 16-week duration recommended in the label (n=1 each for A30K or Y93H).
L409: Most GT3 infected subjects who experienced virologic failure with a GLE/PIB regimen had treatment-emergent RASs in NS3 and NS5A. A variety of different NS3 amino acid substitutions emerged, including Y56H/N, Q80K/R, A156G, and Q186L/R. In NS5A, Y93H was the predominant treatment-emergent resistance pathway, occurring in 15/18 (83%) subjects. Importantly, there was no obvious indication that treatment duration (8-16 weeks) influenced the likelihood of detecting NS3 and NS5A RASs at the time of virologic failure. Therefore, one should expect a high likelihood of treatment-emergent RASs in patients who fail treatment with any GLE/PIB treatment duration ≥8 weeks.

Although data were limited given that there were relatively few subjects with virologic failures (n=19), there was no indication that age, sex or race played a role in these virologic failures.

L415: Source: ADEFP23 database.
L416: Reviewer Comment: Given the totality of data for GT3 TN and TE-PRS subjects in this application, the review team supports the indication of 8 weeks of therapy for treatment naïve patients without cirrhosis, 12 weeks of therapy for treatment naïve subjects with compensated cirrhosis (CTP A liver disease) and 16 weeks of therapy for patients with PRS-treatment experience.
L417: For treatment-naïve, noncirrhotic HCV GT3 infected subjects, the decision to recommend GLE/PIB for 8 weeks versus 12 weeks was a major review issue due to the slightly higher 2% relapse rate observed with the 8 versus 12-week duration. Additional statistical and virology analyses did not identify a specific subgroup that clearly benefited from a 12-week versus 8-week treatment duration. The Applicant also provided additional summary data at the mid-cycle to support the 8 week indication in this population, which included SVR12 results from an additional 21 HCV GT3 infected subjects with HCV/HIV-1 coinfection who received GLE/PIB for 8 weeks in an ongoing trial (EXPEDITION-2, M14-730). All 21 subjects achieved SVR12. In addition, the Applicant reported that 5 HCV GT3 infected subjects who failed treatment with GLE/PIB in Phase 2 trials, were successfully re-treated with SOF-based regimens outside of GLE/PIB clinical trials, addressing the question of possible re-treatment options. The review team therefore concluded that GLE/PIB for 8 weeks for treatment-naïve, noncirrhotic HCV GT3 infected patients is well supported based on the high SVR12 rate, and low relapse rate (&lt;3%) observed in clinical trials, with an adequate sample size (n=186 in NDA who received GLE/PIB for 8 weeks).
L418: The review team concluded that twelve weeks of therapy for treatment naïve cirrhotic subjects is also well supported given the lack of virologic failures in this adequately sized subset (N=65 in the NDA who received GLE/PIB for 12 weeks). The decision to recommend a conservative 16-week GLE/PIB duration for treatment-experienced (IFN- or SOF-based), HCV
L419: GT3 infected patients with or without cirrhosis was made in order to address the risk of relapse which was about 4% higher (8.3% versus 4.5%) in subjects treated with 12- and 16-week GLE/PIB durations, respectively.
L420: GENOTYPE 4
L421: Overall Genotype 4(GT4) Data:
L422: As shown in Table 8, there were a total of 178 HCV GT4, NS3/4A PI- and NS5A Inhibitor-Naïve subjects in the Phase 2/3 dataset treated for 8 or 12 week durations. Of these, 20 (11.2%) were subjects with compensated cirrhosis and 122 (68.5%) were treatment naive. Subjects with treatment experience either had INF or P/R experience or experience with SOF and RBV or P/R. No subjects experienced virologic failure. Four subjects did not achieve SVR12 for reasons not related to virologic failure, including missing SVR12 data and discontinuation of treatment.

NC=non-cirrhotic; C=cirrhotic; PRS= treatment-experienced with regimens containing interferon, pegylated interferon, ribavirin, and/or sofosbuvir, PI=protease inhibitor; NS=not studied * Confidence intervals were calculated using the Wilson score method, no CI calculated for group sizes less than 5.
L429: All data for GT4 comes from single arm trials. GT4 subject had high SVR12 rates (92-100%) across the various subgroups and there were no virologic failures. Therefore, a brief summary of the trials and overall SVR results are provided below.
L430: Treatment-Naive and Treatment Experienced-PRS Subjects Without Cirrhosis- 12 week duration
L431: Approximately half of non-cirrhotic GT4 subjects (76/158 or 48%) were enrolled in ENDURANCE-4. This study was a single-arm, open label Phase 3 trial that evaluated the safety and efficacy of 12 weeks GLE/PIB in TN and PRS-TE subjects with HCV GT 4, 5, or 6 infection without cirrhosis. In total, 76 HCV GT4 subjects enrolled into ENDURANCE-4, of which, 45 subjects were TN and 31 subjects were PRS-TE. This single arm trial had no formal hypothesis testing. The number and percentage of subjects in the overall ITT population that achieved SVR12 were summarized and the 95% CI was calculated. Please see the statistical review for the overall assessment of ENDURANCE-4. Seventy-five of 76 GT4 subjects (98.7%) in ENDURANCE-4 achieved SVR12, one GT4 subject withdrew early from the trial due to an adverse reaction of TIA (discussed in detail in Section 8.4.2).
L432: Treatment-Naive and Treatment Experienced-PRS Subjects Without Cirrhosis- 8 week duration
L433: Non-cirrhotic subjects (TN and PRS-TE) enrolled in SURVEYOR-2 received 8 weeks of therapy (Arm 5, GT4 only, n=46). As described above, SURVEYOR-2 was a multi-arm, multi-part, open label Phase 2 trial. There were seven GT4 PRS-TE subjects in this study and 39 TN subjects in this study. There were no virologic failures. SVR 12 was 100% in the TE population and 92% in TN ITT-PS population (4 subjects did not achieve SVR12 due to other reasons).
L434: Treatment-Naive and Treatment Experienced-PRS Subjects With Cirrhosis-12 week duration
L435: Clinical ReviewLarissa Stabinski, MDAimee Hodowanec, MDNDA 209394Mavyret® (glecaprevir and pibrentasvir)
L436: The majority of the GT-4 subjects with cirrhosis (14/16) were enrolled in EXPEDITION-1. As described above, EXPEDITION-1 was a Phase 2, single-arm, open-label, multicenter study to evaluate the safety and efficacy of a 12-week regimen of GLE/PIB for treating GT 1, 2, 4, 5 or 6 HCV-infected adult subjects with compensated cirrhosis who are either TN or TE-PRS. There were 12 GT 4 TN subjects and four TE subjects enrolled in the trial. All subjects achieved SVR12; there were no GT4 virologic failures in this trial.
L437: Clinical Virology Considerations:
L438: No cases of virologic failure occurred among HCV GT4 infected, noncirrhotic subjects who received GLE/PIB for 8 weeks (n=46) or 12 weeks (n=112), or among cirrhotic subjects who received GLE/PIB for 12 weeks (n=20). Although the number of HCV GT4 infected subjects who received the 8-week duration was relatively limited (n=46), the lack of any relapses with this duration, as well as the consistent efficacy overall for HCV GT4 infected subjects, support the use of GLE/PIB for 8 weeks for noncirrhotic, HCV GT4 infected patients.
L439: Several HCV GT4 subtypes were represented, although subtypes 4a and 4d were predominant, consistent with previous DAA trials. Among HCV GT4 infected subjects enrolled at U.S. study sites, 40/47 (85%) had a subtype 4a infection. Given the 0% virologic failure rate for HCV GT4 infected subjects, there was no evidence that baseline polymorphisms in NS3 or NS5A affected treatment outcome.
L440: Reviewer Comment:
L441: Given the high SVR rates and lack of virologic failure in GT4 TN and PRS-TE subjects in this application, the review team supports the indication of 8 weeks of therapy for subjects without cirrhosis and 12 weeks of therapy for subjects with compensated cirrhosis (CTP A liver disease) in this population.
L442: GENOTYPE 5
L443: Overall Genotype 5(GT5) Data:
L444: As shown in Table 8, there were a total of only 32 HCV GT5, TN and TE-PRS subjects in the Phase 2/3 dataset. Of these, 2 (6.3%) were subjects with compensated cirrhosis and 26 (81.3%) were treatment naïve. Subjects with treatment experience either had INF or P/R experience. No subjects experienced virologic failure and all subjects achieved SVR 12.
L445: Statistical Considerations:
L446: Data from ENDURANCE-4 (n=26), SURVEYOR-1 (Part 2)(n=1), SURVEYOR-2 (n=2), EXPEDITION-1 (n=2) and EXPEDITION-4 (n=1) supports GLE/PIB for treatment of TN and PRS-TE HCV GT5 subjects. Overall, GT5 SVR12 data by trial, treatment experience, cirrhosis status and treatment duration are shown in the below table.

L451: The majority of GT5 subjects (26/32) in this application were enrolled in ENDURANCE-4. As described above, ENDURANCE-4 was a single-arm, open label Phase 3 trial that evaluated the safety and efficacy of 12 weeks of GLE/PIB in TN or PRS-TE subjects with HCV GT 4- 6 infection without cirrhosis. In total, there were 26 GT5 TN or PRS-TE subjects enrolled in ENDURANCE-4 and one GT5 subject enrolled in SURVEYOR-1 Part 2; all were treated for 12 weeks. The SVR12 rate was 100%; there were no GT5 virologic failures from either trial. Only 2 non-cirrhotic subjects were enrolled in SURVEYOR-2 and received 8 weeks of therapy. Both subjects achieved SVR12. Similarly, there were 2 GT5 subjects with cirrhosis enrolled in EXPEDITION-1, however, they received 12 weeks of therapy; both subjects achieved SVR12.
L452: Clinical Virology Considerations:
L453: Of the 32 HCV GT5 infected subjects who received GLE/PIB for 8 or 12 weeks in clinical trials, no subjects experienced virologic failure. However, only 2 subjects received GLE/PIB for 8 weeks, and these 2 subjects did not appear to be representative of the broader GT5 population. Both subjects who received the 8-week duration had low baseline HCV RNA levels; these subjects had the second and third lowest HCV RNA levels of all 32 HCV GT5 infected subjects. The other 30 HCV GT5 infected subjects (28 noncirrhotic, 2 with cirrhosis) received GLE/PIB for 12 weeks.
L454: An NS3 D168E polymorphism, which has potential for clinically significant protease inhibitor resistance, was also detected in 13/31 (42%) of HCV GT5a infected subjects with available data, but all 13 subjects received GLE/PIB for 12 weeks.
L455: Reviewer Comment: Given the high SVR12 rate in this population, and the small but adequate
L456: sample size of GT5 TN and PRS-TE subjects studied for 12 weeks, the review team supports the indication of 12 weeks of therapy for patients without cirrhosis and with compensated cirrhosis (CTP A liver disease) in this population. A significant review issue was the adequacy of data to support an 8 week duration for non-cirrhotic GT5 subjects. Given that there were only 2 subjects who were treated for the 8 week duration, and that they did not appear to be representative of the broader GT5 population (neither had a D168 mutation and both had low baseline HCV viral load), the review team did not support this indication. While the Division generally has allowed for some extrapolation of results for lower prevalence HCV GTs like GT5 and GT6, even with the 12-week duration some extrapolation of available data needs to be considered to support treatment recommendations for GT5 -infected patients with compensated cirrhosis or prior sofosbuvir- or interferon-based treatment experience. The sponsor has an ongoing trial, M16-126, that will assess the efficacy of the 8-week duration in additional noncirrhotic subjects with HCV GT5 or GT6 infection. This study could provide sufficient data to support recommending GLE/PIB for 8 weeks for this population with a future submission.
L457: GENOTYPE 6
L458: Overall Genotype 6 (GT6) Data:
L459: As shown in Table 8, there were a total of 48 HCV GT6, TN and TE-PRS subjects in the Phase 2/3 dataset. Of these, 7 (14.6%) were subjects with compensated cirrhosis and 41 (85.4%) were treatment naïve. Subjects with treatment experience either had INF or P/R experience. Subjects were treated with GLE/PIB for 8 or 12 weeks; no subjects experienced virologic failure. One subject did not achieve SVR12 for reasons not related to virologic failure; this subject was missing SVR12 data.
L460: Statistical Considerations:

L467: The majority of non-cirrhotic subjects 56.1% (23/41) were enrolled in ENDURANCE-4. As described above, ENDURANCE-4 was a single-arm, open label Phase 3 trial that evaluated the safety and efficacy of 12 weeks of GLE/PIB in TN or TE subjects with HCV GT 4- 6 infection without cirrhosis. L468: Eight TN noncirrhotic and 2 TE noncirrhotic subjects were enrolled in SURVEYOR-2. Seven (6 TN and 1 TE subjects with cirrhosis) were enrolled in EXPEDITION-1. The single GT6 subject who did not achieve SVR12 (non-virologic failure) was enrolled in SURVEYOR-2.
L469: Clinical Virology Considerations:
L470: HCV GT6 is extremely genetically diverse with at least 28 reported subtypes and wild-type amino acid sequences at multiple key positions in NS5A, including at positions 28, 30 and 93, vary across different subtypes within GT6, and also between GT6 and other HCV genotypes. Based on the limited sample size and SVR data, it is not possible to draw any firm conclusions regarding the potential impact of HCV GT6 genetic diversity or baseline polymorphisms on the efficacy of an 8-week treatment duration of GLE/PIB.
L471: Reviewer Comment: Given the high SVR12 rate in this population, and the small but adequate sample size of GT6 TN and TE-PRS subjects studied for 12 weeks, the review team supports the indication of 12 weeks of therapy for patients without cirrhosis and with compensated cirrhosis (CTP A liver disease) for this population.
L472: Like for GT5, the significant review issue was the adequacy of data to support an 8 week duration for non-cirrhotic subjects. Only 8 non-cirrhotic treatment naïve and 2 INF or P/R treatment experienced subjects who were treated for 8 weeks duration were a part of this application. As stated above, while the Division has allowed for some extrapolation of results for lower prevalence HCV GTs like GT5 and GT6, even with the 12-week duration some extrapolation of available data needs to be considered to support treatment
L473: Clinical ReviewLarissa Stabinski, MDAimee Hodowanec, MDNDA 209394Mavyret® (glecaprevir and pibrentasvir)
L474: recommendations for GT6-infected patients with compensated cirrhosis or prior sofosbuvir- or interferon-based treatment experience. The ongoing trial, M16-126, addressing the efficacy of the 8-week duration in additional subjects with HCV GT5 or GT6 infection, may provide data to potentially support an 8 week duration of GLE/PIB for GT 5 or 6 patients without cirrhosis in the future.
L475: 6.2. MAGELLAN-1 (M15-410)
L476: 6.2.1. Study Design
L477: Overview and Objective
L478: MAGELLAN-1 was a multi-arm, multi-part, open label Phase 2 trial that evaluated GLE/PIB, co-administered or fixed-dose combination, with or without RBV in adults with HCV GT 1, 4, 5, and 6 infection who failed a prior anti-HCV DAA-containing regimen. The primary objectives in Part 1 of this study were to assess the efficacy and safety of ABT-493 and ABT-530, an the contribution of RBV in adults with chronic HCV GT1 infection who previously failed treatment with a DAA-containing regimen. The arm(s) in Part 1 of the study, which met the pre-specified efficacy and safety criteria, were studied in Part 2 of the study, where the efficacy and safety of the regimen were confirmed by evaluating it in a broader and larger subject population.
L479: Trial Design
L480: The trial contained two parts, with Part 2 initiated based on achieving safety and efficacy criteria in Part 1. Those with HCV GT 4, 5, or 6 were included in Part 2 only. Only data from Arm C (N=22) in Part 1 and Arms D (N=44) and E (N=47) in Part 2 used the selected Phase 3 dosage and thus were submitted for review. Randomization occurred in a 1:1 ratio between Arms D and E. Randomization was stratified by HCV genotype (GT1 or GT4 – GT6) and by previous experience to the following 2 DAA regimen classes:
L481: 1. NS5A inhibitor (\pm PI)-experienced, limited to DCV-, LDV-, or OBV-containing combination regimens; or2. NS5A inhibitor-naive/NS3/4A PI-experienced, limited to: PTV/r-, SMV-, TVR-, or BOC-containing combination regimens.
L482: This study was not designed to test a formal hypothesis, although hypothesis testing is conducted. See Figure 1 below.
L483: Figure 1 M15-610 MAGELLAN-1 Trial Design
L484: Clinical Review
  Larissa Stabinski, MD
  Aimee Hodowanec, MD
  NDA 209394
  Mavyret® (glecaprevir and pibrentasvir)
L485: Figure 1. Study M15-410 Schematic
L486: Study M15-410 Schematic diagram showing two parts of the study. Part 1 (Treatment Period D1 to W12) includes Arm A (N=20), Arm B (N=20), and Arm C (N=20) for HCV GT1 DAA-experienced/naïve subjects. Part 2 (Treatment Period D1 to W16) includes Arm D (N=40) and Arm E (N=40) for HCV GTs 1 and 4-6 DAA-experienced subjects. A decision box between Part 1 and Part 2 states 'Pre-specified efficacy and safety criteria are met*'. Arm A: ABT-493 200 mg QD + ABT-530 80 mg QD. Arm B: ABT-493 300 mg QD + ABT-530 120 mg QD + RBV 800 mg QD. Arm C: ABT-493 300 mg QD + ABT-530 120 mg QD. Arm D: ABT-493/ABT-530 300/120 mg QD. Arm E: ABT-493/ABT-530 300/120 mg QD.
L487: *Refer to Protocol Section 5.4.1.3 for further details.
L488: D = day; DAA = direct-acting antiviral agent; GT = genotype; HCV = hepatitis C virus; RBV = ribavirin; QD = once daily; W = week
L489: Source: Magellan-1 protocol
L490: Study Endpoints
L491: The primary efficacy endpoint was the percentage of subjects who achieved SVR12 (HCV RNA &lt; LLOQ 12 weeks after the last actual dose of study drug).
L492: Statistical Analysis Plan
L493: For each treatment arm, the number and percentage of subjects achieving SVR12 were summarized along with a 95% confidence interval using the Wilson score. In addition, the difference in SVR12 rates between treatment arms was analyzed using the stratum-adjusted Mantel-Haenszel proportion with a continuity correction for variance, adjusting for each of the randomization stratum. Data from Arms A and B are included in total calculations, but are not presented separately as they did not use the proposed dose of GLE/PIB.
L494: 6.2.2. Study Results
L495: Patient Disposition
L496: A total of 50 subjects were randomized and received \geq 1 dose of study drug in Part 1. Two subjects discontinued study drug prematurely (1 subject due to virologic failure and 1 subject was lost to follow-up). In Part 2 of the study, a total of 91 subjects were randomized and received \geq 1 dose of study drug in Part 2. Four subjects (all in Arm E) discontinued study drug prematurely due to virologic failure.
L497: Clinical Review
  Larissa Stabinski, MD
  Aimee Hodowanec, MD
  NDA 209394
  Mavyret® (glecaprevir and pibrentasvir)
L498: Protocol Violations/Deviations
L499: A total of 3 protocol deviations occurred for 3 subjects. Two were violations of exclusion criteria and one subject took a prohibited medication (Senna Plus/Sennosides SOD) containing an herbal derivative from Days 35-37. These protocol violations had no bearing on the interpretability of the trial results.

L506: Reviewer Comment: The vast majority [137/141 (97%)] of subjects were infected with HCV genotype 1 and therefore only genotype 1 was considered for an indication by the FDA review team. One-third, 33%, of the subjects were both NS5A and NS3/4A PI-experienced. A little more than a third, 37%, of subjects were NS5A-naïve and NS3/4A PI-experienced and 30% were NS5A-experienced and NS3/4A PI-naïve. Approximately 19% of the subjects had cirrhosis.
L507: Mean age was approximately 56 years at base and approximately three quarters of the study population was white or male. Because subject randomization occurred within Parts 1 and 2 separately, it is expected that Arms D and E have balanced distributions of characteristics and demographics and most of the characteristics are balanced between Arms D and E. However, Arm E had a lower percentage of cirrhotic subjects, a slightly higher mean log-10 HCV RNA at baseline, and a slightly higher percentage of NS5A-experienced subjects compared to Arm D.

Six subjects experienced on-treatment virologic failure, five subjects experienced post-treatment relapse, and two subjects had missing SVR12 values. Ten of the subjects with virologic failure were GT1a, and one additional subject was GT1b. Eight of the 11 subjects were both NS3/4A PI- and NS5A-experienced.

L519: Virology Analysis
L520: Complementary, but slightly different from the statistics approach shown above, the Clinical Virology reviewer pooled subjects who received GLE/PIB for 12 weeks across Arms A, C and D since the only differences in treatments administered were the DAA formulations used and the slightly lower GLE and PIB dose levels administered in Arm A (n=6, all achieved SVR12). Arm B subjects received GLE/PIB plus RBV for 12 weeks, and Arm E subjects received GLE/PIB for 16 weeks.
L521: Using this approach, overall SVR12 rates ranged from 86-100% across treatment arms. Virologic failure occurred in 10/107 (9%) and 1/26 (4%) subjects with HCV GT1a or GT1b infection, respectively (non-VF-censored population). All 11 subjects who experienced virologic failure had treatment experience with an NS5A inhibitor-containing regimen. Results on the
L522: impact of cirrhosis status on treatment outcome were inconclusive due to limited data, particularly when accounting for GLE/PIB treatment regimen and prior DAA class experience.
L523: Among HCV GT1 infected subjects who were NS3/4A PI-experienced and NS5A inhibitor-naïve, GLE/PIB for 12 weeks resulted in an SVR12 rate of 26/26 (100%, non-VF-censored), with no obvious benefit of dosing GLE/PIB for &gt;12 weeks or in combination with RBV, supporting the use of GLE/PIB for 12 weeks in this population.
L524: Among HCV GT1 infected, NS5A inhibitor-experienced, NS3/4A PI-naïve subjects, non-VF-censored SVR12 rates were 90% (18/20) and 94% (16/17) for those who received GLE/PIB for 12 or 16 weeks, respectively. Efficacy was lower for those with prior NS5A inhibitor and NS3/4A PI experience, with SVR12 rates of 83% (19/23) and 80% (12/15) with the 12- and 16-week durations, respectively. Although SVR12 rates were similar with the 12- and 16-week durations, all 4 instances of virologic failure with the 16-week duration occurred on-treatment, while all instances of relapse occurred among subjects who received the 12-week duration, providing some evidence that a 16-week duration may reduce the risk of relapse. In general, the numbers of subjects who received GLE/PIB + RBV were insufficient to determine if RBV improves treatment efficacy.
L525: Prior NS5A inhibitor experience was associated with the presence of baseline NS5A resistance-associated substitutions (RASs) that either existed as natural baseline amino acid polymorphisms or emerged from prior NS5A inhibitor experience. Virologic failure with GLE/PIB treatment was associated with the presence of baseline NS5A RASs, particularly at position Q30 or at multiple NS5A positions, and baseline NS3 D168 RASs were also associated with virologic failure for subjects with prior NS5A inhibitor and NS3/4A PI experience. Most virologic failure subjects had treatment-emergent or evolving RASs in NS3 or NS5A, or in both targets, indicating the potential for further treatment-emergent resistance selection with GLE/PIB failure. Please see virology review for further details.
L526: Reviewer Comment: Based on the above statistical and virology analysis, the review team supports a 12 week treatment indication for NS3/4A PI-experienced and NS5A inhibitor-naïve patients with GT1 HCV infection. An important review issue related to the MAGELLAN-1 trial was if the sponsor had sufficient evidence to support a treatment recommendation for HCV GT1 infected, NS5A inhibitor-experienced patients. Based on the analysis shown above, the review team concluded that the data supports a treatment recommendation for GLE/PIB for 16 weeks for HCV GT1 infected patients who are NS5A inhibitor-experienced and NS3/4A PI-naïve. In particular, this represents a RBV-free, 2DAA treatment regimen for patients who currently have no approved treatment options, which is also important for patients with moderate to severe renal failure who cannot take a SOF-based regimen. However, the data were not supportive of a treatment recommendation for GT1 infected patients with
L527: experience to both NS5A inhibitors and NS3/4A PIs due to higher rates of treatment failure and the potential for further treatment-emergent resistance selection with GLE/PIB failure in this subpopulation.

L535: 6.3. EXPEDITION-4 (M15-462)
L536: 6.3.1. Study Design
L537: Overview and Objective
L538: EXPEDITION-4 (M15-462) was a single-arm, open-label study to evaluate the efficacy and safety of GLE/PIB in renally-impaired adults with chronic hepatitis C (CHC) virus genotype 1 – 6 infection. The primary objectives of the trial were to evaluate the efficacy and safety of 12 weeks of GLE/PIB in adults with CHC genotype 1-6 infection and chronic renal impairment.
L539: This trial began December 21, 2015 and the last subject's last visit for the primary analysis occurred on September 28, 2016. Enrollment was conducted across 30 sites in Australia, Belgium, Canada, France, Greece, Italy, New Zealand, the United Kingdom, and the United States. At the time of trial initiation, there were no HCV DAA regimens approved for use in patients with severe renal impairment. Subsequently on January 28, 2016, grazoprevir/elbasvir (GZR/EBR) was approved for use in HCV genotype 1 and 4 infected patients with severe renal impairment. There remains an unmet medical need for DAA regimens for treatment of patients with severe renal impairment and HCV genotype 2, 3, 5, or 6 infections.
L540: Trial Design
L541: Subjects enrolled in this single-arm, open label trial received GLE/PIB 300 mg/120 mg daily for 12 weeks and were then followed for an additional 24 weeks. At the time of NDA submission the study was ongoing. The analysis included in the submission is an interim analysis where all subjects have completed at least 12 weeks of follow-up. The study design included virologic stopping criteria, such that the study drug was discontinued if there was 1) a confirmed increase from nadir in HCV RNA (2 consecutive HCV RNA measurements &gt; 1 log10 IU/mL above nadir); or 2) a confirmed HCV RNA ≥ 100 IU/mL after HCV RNA &lt; lower limit of quantification (LLOQ) during treatment.
L542: The 300 mg/120 mg dose was chosen to maximize efficacy and minimize toxicity across HCV genotypes and patient populations. This dose was well tolerated in Phase 2b trials among subjects with genotype 1- 6 infection without severe renal impairment. Further, the regimen
L543: achieved SVR12 in \geq 95\% of subjects in most Phase 2b trial arms. GLE/PIB is minimally excreted from the kidneys and pharmacokinetic studies have shown that renal impairment does not impact drug exposure. Therefore, the use of the same dose of study drug in subjects with and without renal impairment was considered appropriate.
L544: The trial was designed to enroll up to 100 adult subjects with CHC and severe renal impairment. Severe renal impairment was defined as an estimated glomerular filtration rate (eGFR) of &lt; 30 mL/min/1.732. Of these 100 subjects, the Sponsor planned for approximately 80 subjects to require dialysis. Subjects infected with HCV genotypes 1 -6 were eligible for study participation. The Sponsor intended for approximately 50 of the subjects to be infected with genotype 1, while the remaining study population would include a mix of genotype 2, 3, 4, 5, and 6-infected subjects. Treatment-naïve and treatment-experienced subjects were eligible for study participation. However, only prior exposure to interferon, ribavirin, and sofosbuvir was permitted among treatment-experienced subjects. Subjects who had failed prior treatment with a protease inhibitor and/or an NS5A inhibitor were excluded. Additionally, genotype 3-infected patients with any prior HCV treatment experience were excluded from the trial as there were insufficient efficacy data to support a 12 week treatment duration in this population.
L545: The study included the enrollment of subjects with and without compensated cirrhosis. Cirrhosis was defined as any one of the following: 1) liver biopsy showing cirrhosis (e.g., Metavir fibrosis score &gt; 3 or Ishak score &gt; 4); 2) FibroTest® score \geq 0.75 and an aspartate aminotransferase: platelet ratio index (APRI) &gt; 2 during screening; or 3) Fibroscan® result \geq 14.6 kPa. Any subject with signs or symptoms of hepatic decompensation or a Child-Pugh score &gt; 6 was excluded from the trial. The sponsor intended for approximately 25 of the enrolled subjects to be cirrhotic.
L546: Subjects with a history of hepatocellular carcinoma, active hepatitis B coinfection (i.e. positive hepatitis B surface antigen), HIV coinfection, coinfection with multiple HCV genotypes, previous solid organ transplantation, any uncontrolled medical conditions, acute renal failure within prior three months, or any cause of liver disease other than CHC were excluded from study participation.
L547: Overall, the Division was in agreement with the trial design as described above.
L548: Study Endpoints
L549: The primary efficacy endpoint was SVR12. This was defined as HCV RNA &lt; \text{LLOQ} 12 weeks after the last actual dose of study drug. The secondary efficacy endpoints were on treatment virologic failure and post-treatment relapse. .
L550: Clinical ReviewLarissa Stabinski, MDAimee Hodowanec, MDNDA 209394Mavyret® (glecaprevir and pibrentasvir)
L551: Statistical Analysis Plan
L552: The primary efficacy and safety analyses were performed on the intent-to-treat (ITT) population. This included all enrolled subjects who received at least one dose of study drug. The number and proportion of subjects achieving SVR12 were determined and a two-sided 95% confidence interval (CI) was calculated. For subjects missing visit values, backward imputation was applied for the analysis of SVR when possible. If backward imputation was not possible, then an HCV RNA value from a local laboratory was imputed if available. If neither of these approaches to imputation were possible, the subject was imputed as a failure. No formal hypothesis was tested in this single arm trial. Please refer to statistics review of Dr. Laree Tracy and Dr. Therri Usher for complete details.
L553: Protocol Amendments
L554: There were two amendments to this protocol. Amendment 1 was received on November 24, 2015. The purpose of this amendment was to exclude treatment-experienced genotype 3 infected subjects due to a lack of established efficacy in this population. The Division agreed that the exclusion of these subjects was appropriate. Amendment 2 was received on March 23, 2016. Notable changes implemented in this amendment include revised contraception use requirements and updated exclusion criteria allowing for the enrollment of subjects on peritoneal dialysis. The changes in Amendment 2 did not significantly affect the conduct of the trial.
L555: 6.3.2. Study Results
L556: Patient Disposition
L557: This study was ongoing at the time of NDA submission. The database was locked on October 7, 2016 and data through the post-treatment week 12 visit was submitted. There were 140 subjects screened and 104 subjects enrolled in EXPEDITION-4. All 104 enrolled subjects received at least one dose of study drug. Therefore all 104 subjects were included in the intention to treat (ITT) population for efficacy analyses and in the safety population for safety analyses. Four subjects discontinued study drug prematurely due to an AE and the remaining 100 subjects completed 12 weeks of study drug as planned. Regarding study disposition, 1 subject discontinued the study prematurely due to death, 2 subjects completed the full 24 weeks of follow up, and the remaining 101 subjects were ongoing in the study at the time of data lock.
L558: Protocol Violations/Deviations
L559: Clinical Review
  Larissa Stabinski, MD
  Aimee Hodowanec, MD
  NDA 209394
  Mavyret® (glecaprevir and pibrentasvir)
L560: Protocol deviations were uncommon in EXPEDITION-4. Six (5.8%) subjects experienced 7 protocol deviations. Six of these deviations pertained to inclusion/exclusion criteria and one deviation pertained to a prohibited concomitant medication.
L561: Reviewer comment: This reviewer agrees with the Applicant that the noted protocol deviations are not likely to have impacted the study results.
L562: Table of Demographic Characteristics
L563: EXPEDITION-4 was a multinational trial, enrolling subjects from 30 sites across 9 countries. Subjects were predominately male (76%) and the median age was 57 years. As intended, approximately half of the subjects were infected with HCV genotype 1 and the remaining subjects were distributed across genotypes 2-6, with only a single genotype 5 subject and a single genotype 6 subject enrolled. Nearly 20% of subjects were cirrhotic, and just over 80% of subjects were on dialysis. See Table 22 for additional details regarding subject demographics and baseline characteristics.

L570: Efficacy Results - Primary Endpoint
L571: Overall SVR12 results are summarized in the below table. Of 104 subjects in the ITT population, 102 (98.1%) achieved SVR12. Among the two subjects who failed to achieve SVR12, 1 subject had missing data (subject died from intracerebral hemorrhage in the post-treatment follow-up period before reaching SVR12 visit) and one subject had discontinued treatment on Day 27 due to an adverse event (Grade 2 diarrhea). There were no virologic failures.

L575: In addition to the primary efficacy endpoint of SVR12, this trial included three key secondary efficacy endpoints: number of subjects experiencing on-treatment virologic failure and number of subjects experiencing relapse by post-treatment Week 12. No subjects met either secondary efficacy endpoint.
L576: Clinical ReviewLarissa Stabinski, MDAimee Hodowanec, MDNDA 209394Mavyret® (glecaprevir and pibrentasvir)
L577: Reviewer comment: Based on the findings of this trial, GLE-PIB for 12 weeks appears highly efficacious in subjects with severe renal impairment regardless of HCV genotype, cirrhosis status, or HCV treatment history. It should be noted that many of these subgroups contained very small numbers of subjects (particularly for genotypes 5 and 6 and for prior sofosbuvir-experienced subgroups). However, based on the observed SVR12 data, it is not anticipated that the presence or absence of renal impairment will impact SVR12 rates; therefore, recommendations for treatment durations in the various subgroups will be made based on the overall data for each particular subgroup.
L578: Subgroup Analyses of the Primary Endpoint
L579: Subgroup analyses were performed to evaluate the impact of various demographic and baseline disease characteristics on efficacy. Both HCV disease characteristics and CKD disease characteristics, including HCV genotype, cirrhosis status, and HCV treatment experience were assessed. Please refer to the statistics review for the results of the subgroup analyses. The two subjects who failed to achieve SVR12 were both HCV genotype 1 infected and both were cirrhotic and on dialysis at enrollment.
L580: Reviewer comment: Given that there were only 2 subjects who did not achieve SVR12, it is not possible to draw any conclusions regarding potential differences in efficacy across the various subgroups.
L581: 7 Integrated Review of Effectiveness
L582: 7.1. Assessment of Efficacy Across Trials
L583: 7.1.1. Primary Endpoints
L584: Results the Phase 2/3 dataset from were pooled to compile a summary table describing SVR12 rates by HCV genotype, treatment experience, for the FDA recommended treatment durations. GLE/PIB is highly effective across all 6 evaluated HCV GTs, in treatment experienced subjects with prior exposures to INF, P/R, SOF and RBV or P/R, and in GT1 subjects with prior exposure to NS5A or NS3/4A PI inhibitors.

L591: 7.1.2. Subpopulations
L592: SVR12 in the Phase 2/3 dataset did not vary substantially based on age, race, or sex. As previously noted, there were very few treatment failures across the trials, and the small numbers complicated attempts to identify meaningful trends based on age, race, or sex. SVR12 in subjects with severe renal impairment was high and did not differ from SVR in the overall Phase 2/3 dataset.
L593: 7.1.3. Dose and Dose-Response
L594: In Phase 2 dose-ranging studies, lower virologic failure rates were observed in GT3-infected subjects receiving the GLE + PIB 300 mg + 120 mg dose compared to those in lower GLE and PIB dose groups (i.e., 200 mg + 120 mg and 200 mg + 40 mg (Study M14-868). Including RBV in the GLE/PIB regimen did not improve the SVR rate based on SVR12 results in GLE and PIB, and therefore, RBV containing treatment arms were not considered for an indication.
L595: 7.1.4. Onset, Duration, and Durability of Efficacy Effects
L596: The goal of HCV treatment is total viral eradication, as measured by SVR. Therefore the duration of therapy needed before HCV viral load becomes undetectable therapy (the onset of efficacy) is less important than maintenance of an undetectable viral load off therapy, which is measured as SVR12 and SVR24. Historically, SVR24 was considered a cure and was used as the regulatory endpoint. However, data from 15 phase 2 and 3 clinical HCV trials demonstrated high correlation between SVR12 and SVR24, thereby prompting DAVP to use SVR12 as the primary endpoint for marketing applications rather than SVR24[9]. Complete SVR24 data were not available for all the Phase 2/3 trials but these data will be submitted upon trial completion.
L597: 7.2. Additional Efficacy Considerations
L598: 7.2.1. Considerations on Benefit in the Postmarket Setting
L599: GLE/PIB would be the first DAA HCV treatment regimen that yields high SVR rates across HCV GT 1-6 with an indicated treatment duration as short as 8 weeks for non-cirrhotic, treatment-naive or PRS-experienced patients with GT 1-4. This represents a large portion of the US population with chronic HCV. GLE/PIB would also be the first all-DAA regimen indicated in the population of subjects with severe renal impairment for genotypes 2, 3, 5 and 6. In addition GLE/PIB, provides an important DAA-only, RBV-free treatment option for GT1-infected patients with prior treatment experience with NS5A (16 weeks duration) or NS3/4A PIs (12 weeks duration).
L600: 7.3. Integrated Assessment of Effectiveness
L601: The efficacy of GLE/PIB for the treatment of CHC infection in subjects without cirrhosis and with compensated (CTP A) cirrhosis has been established by the results of the nine pivotal Phase 2 and 3 trials discussed in Section 6. Overall SVR12 rates in the Phase 2 /3 trials were 91 to 100% across all genotypes for the recommended treatment durations of 8 to 16 weeks, as shown above.
L602: Significant efficacy review issues included the duration of treatment (8 versus 12 weeks) for GT3 treatment naïve subjects without cirrhosis, traditionally viewed as the most difficult to treat genotype. However, pooled SVR12 rates (non-VF-censored) were high for both durations 97% (177/183) and 99% (258/261) for those who received GLE/PIB for 8 or 12 weeks, respectively, with only a 1.9% higher relapse rate (2.7% vs. 0.8%) with the 8-week versus 12-week duration. The review team favored the 8 week duration given the small, and non-statistical, difference in efficacy (only 2 subjects more had virologic relapse, relapse of n=5 vs. n=3 for 8 vs 12 weeks, respectively) and the large numbers of persons that would be needed to be treated at 12 weeks to see the small incremental benefit in overall efficacy. There are also numerous obvious and practical benefits of a shorter treatment duration including overall patient treatment experience, less possibility for drug-drug interactions and related adverse events, as well as the public-health benefits of reduced health system burden, including fewer treatment-related visits.
L603: For GTs 5 and 6, given the high SVR12 rate in this population, and the small but adequate sample size of GT5 NS3/4A PI- and NS5A Inhibitor-Naïve subjects studied for 12 weeks, the review team supported the indication of 12 weeks of therapy for patients without cirrhosis and with compensated cirrhosis (CTP A liver disease) in this population. While the Division generally has allowed for some extrapolation of results for lower prevalence HCV GTs like GT5 and GT6, even with the 12-week duration, some extrapolation of available data needed to be considered to support treatment recommendations for GT5 -infected patients with compensated cirrhosis or prior sofosbuvir- or interferon-based treatment experience. The sponsor has an ongoing trial, M16-126, that will assess the efficacy of the 8-week and 12-week durations in treatment-naïve and treatment-experienced-PRS subjects with HCV GT5 or GT6 infection, with and without compensated cirrhosis. This study could provide sufficient data to support a future recommendation of GLE/PIB for 8 weeks for treatment-naïve and treatment-experienced PRS patients without cirrhosis.
L604: The review team recommended a 12 week treatment indication for NS3/4A PI-experienced and NS5A inhibitor-naïve patients with GT1 HCV infection, based on a high SVR12. However, an important review issue related to the MAGELLAN-1 trial was whether the Applicant had provided sufficient evidence to support a treatment recommendation for HCV GT1 infected,
L605: Clinical ReviewLarissa Stabinski, MDAimee Hodowanec, MDNDA 209394Mavyret® (glecaprevir and pibrentasvir)
L606: NS5A inhibitor-experienced patients. The review team concluded that the data support a treatment recommendation for GLE/PIB for 16 weeks for HCV GT1 infected, NS5A inhibitor-experienced, NS3/4A PI-naïve patients, based on the non-VF-censored SVR12 of 94% in this population, when treated for 16 weeks. However, the data were not supportive of a treatment recommendation for GT1 infected patients with experience to both NS5A inhibitors and NS3/4A PIs, due to higher rates of treatment failure and lower SVR12 in this subpopulation (SVR 80%).
L607: GLE-PIB for 12 weeks also appeared highly efficacious in subjects with severe renal impairment regardless of HCV genotype, cirrhosis status, or HCV treatment history. It should be noted in the trial of subjects with severe renal impairment many of the genotype and patient subpopulations contained very small numbers of subjects (particularly for genotypes 5 and 6 and for prior sofosbuvir-experienced subgroups). However, based on both the metabolism of GLE/PIB and the observed SVR12 data, it is not anticipated that the presence or absence of renal impairment will impact SVR12 rates, therefore recommendations for treatment durations in the various genotypes and subpopulations of patients with severe renal impairment will follow the overall data supported indication for each particular genotype or subgroup.
L608: SVR12 was 100% with no virologic failures GT1 HCV/HIV co-infected subpopulation. As it is also not anticipated that the presence or absence of HIV infection will impact SVR12 rates, recommendations for treatment durations in the various genotypes and subpopulations of patients with HIV-co-infection will follow the overall data for each particular genotype or subgroup.
L609: In summary, this is the first regimen with pan-genotypic efficacy, which can be used for as little as 8 weeks in non-cirrhotic, treatment-naïve subjects with GT 1-4 infection, or in non-cirrhotic, PRS-treatment-experienced subjects with GTs 1, 2 or 4. This would also be the first approved regimen of any kind for subjects with HCV GT 2, 3, 5, or 6 infection and severe renal impairment. Additionally, the regimen provides an important treatment option for some HCV GT1 DAA- experienced patients.
L610: 8 Review of Safety
L611: Safety Review Approach
L612: Eight of the nine pivotal Phase 2 and 3 trials were included in the pooled safety population for the majority of safety analyses: ENDURANCE-1 (M13-590), ENDURANCE-2 (M15-464), ENDURANCE-3 (M15-594), ENDURANCE-4 (M13-583), SURVEYOR-1 (M14-867), SURVEYOR-2 (M14-868), EXPEDITION-1(M14-172) and MAGELLAN-1 (M15-410). The Phase 2/Phase3 pooled safety dataset included data from 2,265 subjects with or without cirrhosis who received at least
L613: Clinical ReviewLarissa Stabinski, MDAimee Hodowanec, MDNDA 209394Mavyret® (glecaprevir and pibrentasvir)
L614: one dose of GLE/PIB at the proposed labelling dosage (b) (4) co-formulated GLE 300 mg QD and PIB 120 mg QD without RBV for 8, 12 or 16 weeks. The pooled safety database excluded subjects with severe renal impairment enrolled in EXPEDITION-4 (M15-462), as subjects with severe renal impairment have different baseline health status. Throughout the safety review, the Phase 2/3 safety database will be used to define the total safety population, excluding EXPEDITION-4 subjects (n=2,265) as this trial was reviewed separately and is discussed further below in Section 8.7.
L615: Individual analysis of ENDURANCE-2, the placebo controlled trial, and ENDURANCE-3, the active controlled trial are also presented. The Phase 2/Phase 3 pooled safety dataset was analyzed by duration and cirrhosis status and these data are also presented. A thorough hepatic safety review was conducted given adverse events of hepatic decompensation associated with earlier generation marketed HCV NS3/4A PIs. Deaths occurring in any submitted Phase 2 or Phase 3 trial were reviewed and assessed.
L616: Dr. Lara Stabinski conducted the above safety analyses which are presented in Section 8. Unless otherwise specified, clinical trial data were independently analyzed using the SDTM datasets as well as the ISS datasets in JReview. Any differences in findings by the FDA reviewer compared to the Applicant were relatively minor and attributable to variable methods of pooling and subgroup analyses. All of the safety assessments and conclusions are those of the FDA reviewer unless otherwise specified.
L617: Dr. Aimee Hodowanec conducted the primary safety analysis of EXPEDITION-4, which is presented in Section 8.7. As stated above, EXPEDITION-4 was not pooled with any other trial for analyses presented in Section 8 because subjects with severe renal impairment (CKD Stage 4 or 5) represent a considerably different patient population and thereby have potentially different safety concerns.
L618: The Applicant also submitted a Safety Update Report (SUR) four months after the original NDA submission. Deaths, SAEs, potential cases of hepatotoxicity, hepatic decompensation and events of hepatocellular carcinoma, reported in the SUR are included in the relevant safety sections.

L638: 8.2.3. Adequacy of the safety database:
L639: The safety database for both products is comprehensive and adequate to assess the safety of GLE/PIB for the proposed indication, dosage regimen, duration of treatment, and patient population. The Phase 2/3 trials evaluated over 2,300 subjects treated at the proposed dose and duration of GLE/PIB, which exceeds FDA’s recommendation for a 1000-1500 subject safety database for treatment of patients with compensated liver disease.[10]
L640: 8.3. Adequacy of Applicant’s Clinical Safety Assessments
L641: 8.3.1. Issues Regarding Data Integrity and Submission Quality
L642: No data quality or data integrity issues were identified. For Phase 2 and 3 trials, all narratives for deaths, SAEs, and treatment discontinuations were reviewed and compared to the Applicant’s summary and assessment.
L643: 8.3.2. Categorization of Adverse Events
L644: No issues were identified with respect to recording, coding, and categorizing of adverse events (AEs). The Applicant categorized AEs and serious adverse events (SAEs) in accordance with standard regulatory definitions. AEs were defined in Common Terminology Criteria for Adverse Events (CTCAE). The Investigator rated the severity of each AE according to the National Cancer Institute (NCI)-Common Terminology Criteria for Adverse Events (CTCAE) Version 4. The clinical reviewers verified the Applicant’s translation of verbatim terms to preferred terms for events reported in the Phase 2/3 Safety dataset.
L645: 8.3.3. Routine Clinical Tests

L669: Reviewer Comment: AEs, SAEs and Grade 3 or higher AEs occurred at higher frequency in groups with expected poorer baseline health status including in the study of severe renal impairment, in those with cirrhosis and those receiving 16 weeks of therapy (who were more likely to have cirrhosis, HCV genotype 3 and to be treatment experienced). However, AEs considered by the Applicant/Investigator as possibly related occurred at more similar frequencies across these subgroups; DAA-related AEs occurred at similar frequencies to AEs in the placebo and active control arms. There was only one SAE and one Grade 3 or higher AE considered by the Applicant to be possibly related to the study drug. The overall AE summary is favorable with few subjects discontinuing GLE/PIB due to AEs (0.5% overall).

L701: Reviewer comment: Overall, acute infections comprised a largest number of the treatment emergent SAEs observed, which are unlikely to be related to either the underlying disease condition (in this population with well compensated liver disease) or study medication.
L702: Numbers in the Neoplasms SOC were driven by hepatocellular carcinoma in the cirrhotic sub-population. These 3 hepatocellular carcinomas represent 3 of 6 total hepatocellular carcinoma cases seen in the GLE/PIB arms of this study (3 cases were not classified as treatment emergent SAEs). All hepatocellular carcinoma cases are discussed in detail in Section 8.5.1. In brief, hepatocellular carcinoma is a known complication of chronic HCV infection, usually seen in patients with cirrhosis. The effect of DAA therapy on the development or prevention of hepatocellular carcinoma is not completely understood; however in this study of well compensated cirrhotics (mean CTP 5.1) successful therapy with GLE/PIB did not eliminate the risk of hepatocellular carcinoma.
L703: Other than hepatocellular carcinoma, “transient ischemic attack” was the only one other Dictionary Defined Term Treatment Emergent SAE that occurred in more than one subject. One of these events, was the only SAE considered possibly related to study drug by the Investigator. Both subjects and their SAEs are described briefly below.
L704: • Subject 303402 is a 49 year old non-cirrhotic white male with a history of smoking cigarettes (0.5 packs/day for 30 years), obesity (baseline BMI 31), and cardiac conduction abnormality on Screening electrocardiogram (ECG), and elevated hemoglobin (18.1 g/dL) and hematocrit at Screening, experienced two events of transient ischemic attack (TIA) on Day 11 and 36. On Day 11, the subject experienced right hand clumsiness and Broca’s aphasia. On Day 11, a carotid ultrasound, cerebral magnetic resonance imaging
L705: Clinical ReviewLarissa Stabinski, MDAimee Hodowanec, MDNDA 209394Mavyret® (glecaprevir and pibrentasvir)
L706: (MRI), cerebral computerized tomography, and ECG were within normal limits. Study drug was withdrawn, with the last dose administered on Day 12. The subject was treated with enoxaparin and acetylsalicylic acid and experienced another event of dysarthria on Day 36. Blood pressure readings were not provided for the duration of the events, but the subject was normotensive at screening and baseline with a baseline with a blood pressure of 127/80. At Week 2 the subject had an elevated diastolic blood pressure of 133/93 (no further readings were available as the subject discontinued treatment). Cerebral MRI was normal. The Investigator considered the events of TIA as having a reasonable possibility of being related to study drug. The Sponsor considered the events as having no reasonable possibility of being related to study drug, and more likely related to a history of obesity, smoking, and a cardiac conduction abnormality as noted on Screening ECG, as well as elevated hemoglobin and hematocrit.
L707: Reviewer Comment: The reviewer agrees with the Investigator that the younger age, race, elevated diastolic blood pressure (albeit a single reading) on treatment and temporal association between onset of study medication and onset of TIA are suggestive for an adverse drug reaction; although this subject had some possible baseline stroke risk factors including smoking (the baseline conduction abnormality was not mentioned on subsequent ECGs). According to recent studies, the risk of death from ischemic heart disease and stroke doubles with every sustained 20 mm Hg systolic or 10 mm Hg diastolic increase among people from age 40 to 89.[11]
L708: • Subject 326104 is a 62 year old non-cirrhotic Asian female with relevant medical history of right carotid transient ischemic attack approximately 8 months prior (b) (6) and fatty liver, experienced a transient ischemic attack. On Day 4, the subject experienced a sudden onset of dysphagia, difficult speech, and right upper arm weakness for a few minutes and was admitted to the hospital. Computed tomography and magnetic resonance imaging showed no acute abnormalities. Color Doppler sonography of the extracranial carotid showed intimal thickening and sparse mural plaques along the course of bilateral extracranial common and internal carotid arterial and vertebral artery; there was no identifiable significant stenosis. Cardiac echo showed left ventricle diastolic dysfunction or aging process, no intra-cardiac shunt or mass detected, mild mitral reflux, tricuspid regurgitation, pulmonic regurgitation, and no mention of intra-cardiac thrombus. Blood pressure readings were not available during this event but the subject was normotensive at baseline and all subsequent visits; she did have elevated diastolic blood pressure on her screening visit. The subject was treated with acetylsalicylic acid, piracetam, and mecobalamin. The subject's upper limb weakness and speech function improved on the following day after the event. Study drug was not interrupted or discontinued due to this event. The Investigator considered the event of
L709: Clinical ReviewLarissa Stabinski, MDAimee Hodowanec, MDNDA 209394Mavyret® (glecaprevir and pibrentasvir)
L710: transient ischemic attack as having no reasonable possibility of being related to study drug and more likely related to medical history of right carotid transient ischemic attack. The Sponsor considered the event as having no reasonable possibility of being related to study drug and more likely related to cerebrovascular disease and her personal history of previous TIA.
L711: Reviewer Comment: Although the timing of this event is suggestive of an adverse drug reaction, this event occurred in a subject with a recent history of transient ischemic attack (within one year). This reviewer agrees with the Investigator and Applicant that there is no evidence that this event was related to study medication.
L712: In EXPEDITION-4; M15-462, the study population with severe renal impairment, one subject experienced a cerebral hemorrhage resulting in death and one subject experienced facial paresis. Both subjects experienced on-treatment increases and fluctuations in blood pressure proximal to their events, but these fluctuations are difficult to interpret in the context of underlying severe renal impairment. (See Section 8.7 for further details).
L713: Given these cardiovascular SAEs, further analysis of cardiac-related SAEs was conducted. Angina coded as “Angina Pectoris” and “Angina Unstable” Treatment Emergent SAEs occurred in two subjects. They are described briefly below.
L714: Subject 7010 experienced Angina Pectoris. This subject is a 51 year old white male with hypertension noted at screening (150/75) and baseline (169/79) and a history of intermittent chest pain within a year of treatment, who presented on Day 9 -10 with stabbing chest pain, tingling in left arm and headache. In the hospital on Day 10, blood pressure was 160/80 mm/Hg (not clear if this was before or after anti-hypertensives were administered), troponin T level was 5 NG/L (reference range: 0 – 14 NG/L), and an exercise tolerance test (ETT) showed some ST depression noted at late stage during ETT, 1 – 2 mm stage III onwards, with no limiting chest pain; angiogram was normal. Study drug was not interrupted or discontinued due to this event. On Day 14, the subject was discharged from the hospital and the event of angina pectoris was considered resolved. Blood pressure at the 2 week visit (Day 15) was 186/84 but decreased to his baseline at subsequent visits. His hypertension was treated after his event with two antihypertensive drugs (metoprolol day 10-14 and felodipine day 10 -ongoing). The Investigator and Applicant evaluated the event as unrelated to study drug.
L715: Reviewer Comment: Although the timing of this event is suggestive of an adverse drug reaction and at least one on-treatment measurement showed a worsening of blood pressure around the time of the event, the event occurred in a subject with a recent history of intermittent chest pain (within one year). This reviewer agrees with the Investigator and Applicant that there is limited direct evidence that this event was related to study medication
L716: Clinical ReviewLarissa Stabinski, MDAimee Hodowanec, MDNDA 209394Mavyret® (glecaprevir and pibrentasvir)
L717: and could be related to his underlying poorly controlled (or possibly previously untreated) hypertension.
L718: Subject 172105 experienced Angina Unstable. This subject is 65 year old white male with a relevant history of hypertension (screening blood pressure was 158/96 and baseline blood pressure was 156/111) and psoriatic arthritis. The subject experienced an adverse event of hypokalemia (grade 1) first noted on Day 16. The subject's blood pressure significantly decreased on treatment, notably on the Week 1 visit it was 136/80 and on the Week 4 visit it was 122/88. The subject was on doxazosin and lercanapidine during this time period and there were no noted changes to his antihypertensives. On Day 26, the subject felt chest pain secondary to unstable angina and was urgently admitted to the hospital. Limited laboratory results were negative for myocardial infarction including creatine kinase level of 39 IU/mL (reference range: 39 – 308 IU/mL). No other laboratory results were reported. The subject was treated with enoxaparin, acetylsalicylic acid, clopidogrel, co-diovan, and amlodipine. Electrocardiogram (ECG) showed T-wave abnormality with no sign of myocardial infarction. Coronography showed stenosis of the left anterior descending (LAD) artery and 60 percent stenosis of first diagonal branch. Percutaneous coronary intervention (PCI) of the LAD artery was performed with good circulation observed after PCI. Study drug was not interrupted or discontinued due to this event. The Investigator and Applicant assessed this event as unrelated to the study drug.
L719: Reviewer Comment: Although the dramatic fluctuation in blood pressure is suggestive of an adverse drug reaction (or drug-drug interaction), the event occurred in a subject with underlying coronary artery disease. This reviewer agrees with the Investigator and Applicant that there is no direct evidence that this event was related to study medication.
L720: In addition, 3 subjects experienced SAEs of “Acute myocardial Infarction” or “Myocardial Infarction” coded as non-Treatment Emergent occurring on Study Days 116-132, Post Treatment Days 32-76. There was also one subject who experienced a non-Treatment Emergent SAE of “Coronary Artery Disease” on Day 132 or Post Treatment Day 38. With the exception of one subject with myocardial infarction, all had known histories of cardiovascular disease.
L721: Reviewer Comment: The subject with the “Coronary Artery Disease” event experienced an AE of hypertension on treatment, but overall there was no clear pattern preceding these non-treatment emergent events.
L722: Safety Update:
L723: One additional Subject 8719 was reported as having a treatment emergent SAE of hiatal hernia. The subject is a 61 year old white female, with ongoing alcohol use (less than 2 drinks per day), former smoker, with relevant history of chronic hepatitis C virus infection without cirrhosis,
L724: Clinical ReviewLarissa Stabinski, MDAimee Hodowanec, MDNDA 209394Mavyret® (glecaprevir and pibrentasvir)
L725: hiatal hernia, gastritis, esophagitis, diffuse abdominal tenderness, nausea, bloating and belching, vomiting, and gastroesophageal reflux disease experienced an event of hiatus hernia. She also developed multiple treatment emergent gastrointestinal side effects including abdominal pain, nausea, vomiting, fatigue and dizziness starting on Day 1 of treatment.
L726: Reviewer Comment: The most common cause of a hiatal hernia is an increase in pressure in the abdominal cavity. Pressure can come from vomiting or abdominal distension. Therefore the subject's on treatment clinical course including multiple gastrointestinal symptoms could be reasonably partially attributable to the development of the SAE of hiatal hernia, but is confounded by her history of gastrointestinal illness and pre-existing hernia.
L727: 8.4.2. Dropouts and/or Discontinuations Due to Adverse Effects
L728: Overall, discontinuations due to AEs were infrequent. All provided narratives were reviewed for discontinuations due to AEs. Overall 7 subjects, 0.3% of those in the Phase 2/3 safety dataset, excluding those in the population with severe renal disease, discontinued due to any AE. Of these, three subjects (0.1%) prematurely discontinued study medication due to AE considered related to study drug (Subjects 142305, 303402, 1004020). See Table 32 for summary of all adverse events in subjects with discontinuations due to adverse events.

L734: 8.4.3. Significant Adverse Events
L735: This section describes Grade 3 and 4 adverse events that occurred in the treatment-emergent period. Some of these events were also considered SAEs; hence, there is some overlap between events reported in this section and in section 8.4.2. A total of 67 (3.0%) subjects in the Phase 2/3 safety dataset, excluding subjects with severe renal disease, experienced Treatment Emergent Grade 3 or Grade 4 Adverse Events; 62 (2.7%) experienced Grade 3 events and 10 (0.4%) experienced Grade 4 events (some subjects experiencing both Grade 3 and 4 events). A larger proportion of cirrhotics, 7.6% experienced Grade 3 or 4 AEs. Approximately 60% of the Treatment Emergent Grade 3 and 4 events were SAEs(See section 8.4.2). A total of four subjects experienced Grade 3 or 4 AEs considered by the Investigator as possibly related to study drug; none of these events were considered SAEs, all events resolved and study drug was not discontinued.

In addition, both cases coded as gastroenteritis, particularly the case with several weeks of symptoms, could represent another
L756: gastrointestinal AE, as viral gastroenteritis usually persists a day or 2, occasionally lasting up to 10 days.

L772: Reviewer Comment: Both analyses (all AEs in the placebo controlled trial and ADRs) yield similar results terms, affirming that headache, fatigue, nausea, and diarrhea are the most frequently reported AEs with GLE/PIB. However, the occurrence of some of these events (especially headache and fatigue) was only slightly higher among GLE/PIB subjects, which suggests that the underlying HCV disease state may be contributing to the findings as well. Cirrhotic subjects were also more likely to experience the most common ADRs; however, the types of ADRs were similar to non-cirrhotic subjects. Exploratory analysis confirmed that ADRs were similar across all treatment durations. As shown in Section 10, headache, fatigue, nausea, and diarrhea were reflected in labelling recommendations as the most common ADRs, given that these ADRs were the most common adverse reactions occurring at higher frequency than placebo.

L782: Uric Acid levels were not available for ENDURANCE-2 and most of the subjects in the Phase 2/3 safety studies. However, of 561 non-cirrhotic subjects in studies where this value was routinely collected, 6 subjects (1%) and 13 of 288 cirrhotic subjects (5%) experienced Grade 4 Uric Acid Levels (Grade 4: &gt;10mg/dL). Two non-cirrhotic subjects and four cirrhotic subjects with Grade 4 elevations had normal baseline values (Grade 0 at baseline). Of those with Grade 4 elevations, 2 subjects, both with cirrhosis, experienced non-investigational AEs that could be at least partially attributed to increased levels of uric acid; one subject experienced nephrolithiasis (Week 2, Grade1) and one subject experienced gout(Week 5, Grade 1). Neither event was considered related to GLE/PIB by the Investigator or Applicant, both subjects had confounding Grade 3

L785: Reviewer Comment: Grade 3 and 4 chemistry laboratory abnormalities were uncommon across study groups. ALT and AST trended down rapidly in the active treatment groups as HCV viral load decreased. Hence, it is not surprising that elevated ALT and AST and alkaline phosphatase were observed most frequently among subjects in the placebo group, who had ongoing HCV replication. On the other hand, minor alkaline phosphatase, bilirubin elevations and increases in INR were uncommon, but seen more in the GLE/PIB treatment arm of the placebo controlled study, ENDURANCE -2. Grade 2 and 3 INR elevations were mostly transient with a single spike occurring between TW 1 and 8, associated with a concurrent increase in PTT and but not associated with any obvious bleeding events. Of the subjects with Grade 1 INR elevations, 6 subjects experienced the following events which could reasonably be a consequence of bleeding or hemolysis: Anemia, Hematoma, Ecchymosis, Hematemesis, Blood Urine present, Gout and Blood Uric Acid Increased. No laboratory events were considered appropriate for labelling given the low frequency of abnormalities and lack of clear association with study drug. However, laboratory abnormalities will continue to be monitored for trends in the post marketing setting.

L792: 8.4.6. Vital Signs
L793: Significant fluctuations in blood pressure were observed in several individual subjects. Within the analysis datasets, the sponsor provided flags for subjects experiencing on-treatment extremes in systolic or diastolic blood pressure in the Phase 2/3 safety dataset. The numbers of subjects who met these criteria for at least one study visit, excluding those in EXPEDITION-4
