L800: Treatment Emergent Hypertension
L801: In addition, 42 subjects (1.9%) experienced on-treatment adverse events of hypertension. Twenty four of 42 subjects did not have a previous diagnosis of hypertension. Twenty seven of 42 subjects experienced a grade 1 event of hypertension, 13 subjects experienced a grade 2 event of hypertension and 2 subjects experienced grade 3 events of hypertension. (Subjects 242106 and 174302 who experienced grade 3 events of hypertension and are described in Section 8.43 on Significant Adverse Events.) There were no treatment emergent adverse events of hypertension that were considered serious.
L802: Fourteen subjects with hypertension AEs were considered related to GLE/PIB by the Investigator. These 14 subjects are presented in Appendix C. Of the 14 subjects with hypertension considered related to study drug, 7 had pre-existing hypertension, were on antihypertensive medications, and appeared to experience a worsening of blood pressure on treatment requiring dosage adjustments and/or additional blood pressure medications. Maximum elevation from baseline blood systolic pressure ranged 5 mmHg to 59 mmHg and maximum elevation in diastolic blood pressure ranged from 5 mmHg to 22 mmHg; mean blood pressure elevations and trends were significantly confounded by the treatment of blood pressure.
L803: Seven subjects did not have a previous diagnosis of hypertension and were reported to have new onset hypertension starting as early as Day 8 to Day 29, all were considered ongoing events. Four subjects were started on one or more anti-hypertensives. Maximum elevation from baseline blood systolic pressure ranged 7mmHg to 55mmHg and maximum elevation in diastolic blood pressure ranged from 6mmHg to 23 mmHg; mean blood pressure elevations and trends were significantly confounded by the treatment of blood pressure.
L804: Clinical ReviewLarissa Stabinski, MDAimee Hodowanec, MDNDA 209394Mavyret® (glecaprevir and pibrentasvir)
L805: One non-serious but clinically significant AE of hypertensive heart disease and one clinically significant AE of hypertensive crisis were also reported. The event of hypertensive crisis was considered reasonably related and occurred on Day 39 in a subject (4021) without a previous documented history of hypertension (study vitals are normal indicating that this may have been an event diagnosed by an outside practitioner and treated between the study visits; Day 39 was between 2 study visits and the subject was started on 2 antihypertensive Days 42 and 43 also before returning for a study visit). This subject is also described in Appendix C.
L806: As discussed in Section 8.4.2 (Serious Adverse Events), there are some subjects in which blood pressure fluctuations may have played at least a partial role in precipitating the event including in the subjects listed below:
L807: Subject 303402 303402 Transient Ischemic Attack- 49 year old non-cirrhotic white male, with elevated hemoglobin and ongoing tobacco use, with no known history of hypertension, and normotensive at baseline with a single recorded elevated diastolic blood pressure increase of 13 mmHg proximal to the event of TIA (blood pressure 133/93 - no further readings were available as the subject discontinued treatment). This was the only SAE in the Phase 2/3 dataset with an event considered by the Investigator as related to GLE/PIB.
L808: Subject 7010 Angina Pectoris – 51 year old with baseline hypertension and on moderate on-treatment blood pressure (mainly diastolic) fluctuations (range 101/65 to 142/91). This subject also experienced a myocardial infarction at Week 18-19 (approximately 10 weeks after completing 8 weeks of treatment with GLE/PIB.)
L809: Subject 172105 Angina Unstable – 65 year old white male with baseline hypertension with blood pressure fluctuations on treatment [diastolic range (111mmHg at baseline to 71mmHg) and systolic range (122 mmHg to 160mmHg)]. This subject also experienced hypokalemia.
L810: Given these blood pressure events, an information request (IR) was sent to the Applicant and an blood pressure analysis was conducted including all subjects with the following possible cardiovascular events or symptoms: Events in the Body System or Organ Class Cardiac Disorder or Dictionary Derived Term Chest Pain, Chest Discomfort, Dyspnea, Dyspnea Exertional, Edema, Edema Peripheral, Cerebral Hemorrhage, Facial paresis, Transient Ischemic Attack or Headache.
L811: Of all the subjects in the Phase 2/3 Safety dataset, excluding those with renal failure, 76 subjects experienced these potential cardiovascular events or symptoms defined in this way. Eleven of 76 were previously diagnosed with hypertension, and 40 carried either a diagnosis of hypertension or a medically relevant cardiovascular diagnosis at baseline.
L812: Six of 76 subjects experienced maximum systolic blood pressure elevations of \geq 40 mmHg; 9 subjects experienced maximum systolic elevations between 20-39 mmHg; and 11 experienced systolic elevations between 10-29 mmHg. There were also 8 subjects with maximum diastolic
L813: Clinical ReviewLarissa Stabinski, MDAimee Hodowanec, MDNDA 209394Mavyret® (glecaprevir and pibrentasvir)
L814: elevations &gt;20 mg mmHg and 25 with maximum diastolic elevations of 10-20 mmHg. The majority of subjects with increased blood pressures, particularly at the extremes, carried a previous diagnosis of hypertension or cardiovascular disease, and trends were difficult to discern over time given titration of antihypertensive medications in this population.
L815: Tables describing subjects with these events and maximum systolic blood pressure elevations ≥20mmHg from baseline and diastolic elevations of ≥10mmHg are shown in the Appendix D and E.
L816: Treatment Emergent Hypotension
L817: In addition to the subjects who experienced extreme hypertensive blood pressure values discussed above, adverse events of treatment emergent hypotension were reported for 6 subjects and orthostatic hypotension was reported for one subject. Only the event of orthostatic hypotension was considered causal. No events were considered serious. Of note, of the six subjects with hypotension, 3 subjects (subjects 404403, 700403 and 192104) had baseline hypertension, were on concomitant anti-hypertensive and experienced no other adverse events that could explain their hypotension. The three remaining subjects who experienced hypotension also experienced infections that could reasonably lead to a lowering of blood pressure. The subject with orthostatic hypotension (subject 204205), had a relevant medical history of intestinal occlusion, epigastralgia and Sjogren's syndrome and was on multiple concomitant medications.
L818: Of those with extreme measured values hypotension or reported values of hypotension as AEs, a total of 13 of 57 subjects had baseline hypertensive diagnosis. Most were on concomitant medications including ACE-inhibitors, ARBs, beta blockers and calcium channel blockers. Two subjects experienced other adverse events that could be related to hypotension; the etiology of hypotension in the remaining subjects with a diagnosis of hypertension is not clear.
L819: Overall Reviewer Comment: Overall approximately 84 (3.7%) of subjects experienced either an increased systolic or diastolic blood pressure readings and/or experienced an adverse event of hypertension. In addition, 57 (2.5%) subjects experienced either decreased systolic or diastolic blood pressure readings and/or experienced an adverse event of hypotension or orthostatic hypotension. This could suggest a possible blood pressure signal in both directions. However, the majority of the cases, especially amongst those with increased blood pressure had confounding medical histories and trends were difficult to follow given appropriate medical interventions (i.e. titrating or initiating antihypertensives) for blood pressure control. It is also possible that these values reflect natural variation and/or an unmasking of a chronic disease (particularly hypertension) in the context of the additional clinical scrutiny that subjects receive in a clinical trial.
L820: Clinical ReviewLarissa Stabinski, MDAimee Hodowanec, MDNDA 209394Mavyret® (glecaprevir and pibrentasvir)
L821: Given that several cases of hypotension occurred in subjects with baseline hypertension on anti-hypertensives with no other explanation for low blood pressure, some of the hypotension may be explained by drug-drug interactions, although a specific interaction has not been identified by the review team to date. Similarly, for those with increased blood pressure, the majority of subjects were on concomitant medications at baseline, either for hypertension or other chronic diseases and drug-drug interactions, hepatic metabolism or hepatic transporters could also play a role in treatment emergent hypertension, in this context. Currently, there are no specific data that points to a specific drug-drug interaction, hepatic transporter or other mechanism to explain the hyper and hypo-tension events; additionally, no pre-clinical signals were identified. However, the clinical team continues to work with the clinical pharmacology team to ensure a thorough review of all the available data. If any additional information is discovered that helps to explain these findings, the issue will be updated.
L822: 8.4.7. Electrocardiograms (ECGs)
L823: ECGs were assessed at screening and at the end of treatment. The Applicant reports four subjects with treatment-emergent abnormal ECGs deemed clinically significant by the Investigator:
L824: Subject 167301, a 52-year-old white male, experienced an asymptomatic, non-serious, event of bundle branch block right (Grade 2) that began on Day 85, was considered not related to study drug, and was ongoing at the time of the report. No past cardiovascular medical history and no relevant concomitant medications were reported.
L825: Subject 206503, a 57-year-old white male, had a clinically significant abnormal electrocardiogram finding noted at the End of Treatment Visit (Day 79) of atrial fibrillation with a controlled ventricular response; this finding was recorded as a Grade 1 AE of atrial fibrillation that resolved on Day 96. No other AEs were reported for this subject. No past cardiovascular medical history and no relevant concomitant medications were reported.
L826: Subject 104501, a 58-year-old white male, had QTcF prolongation (498 msec and 497 msec) on Day 85 (1 day after the last dose of study drug) that was unchanged from the screening value of 498 msec. No associated AE was recorded.
L827: Subject 6204, a 66-year-old white male, experienced atrial fibrillation with rapid ventricular response with premature ventricular or aberrantly conducted complexes on Day 58 that were recorded as AEs of atrial fibrillation and defect conduction intraventricular. These events were nonserious, lasted 2 days, and were considered to be not related to study drug. This subject had a history of hypertension, diabetes mellitus, and hyperlipidemia. Baseline electrocardiogram was normal.
L828: All four subjects with ECGs deemed clinically significant by the investigator were either generally asymptomatic at the time of abnormal ECGs and/or had either cardiac risk factors, abnormal screening/baseline ECGs, or findings consistent with age-related risk in this population that confounded the assessment. The primary review team concludes available reported ECG data do not support relevant labeling.
L829: 8.4.8. QT
L830: The effect of fixed dose combination of glecaprevir and pibrentasvir on the QTc interval was evaluated in a Phase 1 randomized, single blind, placebo- and positive-controlled four-period crossover thorough QTc study in 48 healthy adult subjects. The FDA Interdisciplinary Review Team for QT Studies Consultation conducted a thorough QT review of these data. They concluded that the largest upper bounds of the 2-sided 90% CI for the mean difference between fixed-dose combinations (600 mg GLE+240 mg PIB; 400 mg GLE+120 mg PIB) and placebo were below 10 ms, the threshold for regulatory concern as described in ICH E14 guidelines. The largest lower bound of the two-sided 90% CI for the \Delta\DeltaQTcF for moxifloxacin was greater than 5 ms and the moxifloxacin profile over time is adequately demonstrated in the below table, indicating that assay sensitivity was established.
L831: Table 40 The Point Estimates and the 90% CIs Corresponding to the Largest Upper Bounds for ABT-493 400 MG + ABT-530 120 MG and ABT-493 600 MG + ABT-530 240 MG and the Largest Lower Bound for Moxifloxacin 400 mg (FDA Analysis)

L834: In summary, the FDA review team concluded that at 20-fold of glecaprevir and 5-fold of pibrentasvir steady state therapeutic concentrations, the glecaprevir and pibrentasvir combination does not prolong the QTc interval to any clinically relevant extent.
L835: 8.4.9. Immunogenicity
L836: Because GLE and PIB are small molecules and not peptides, immunogenicity was not anticipated and therefore not specifically evaluated in clinical trials.
L837: GLE is an NS3/4A Serine Protease Inhibitor (Chymotrypsin-like). Serine proteases are ubiquitous in eukaryotes and prokaryotes. In humans, serine proteases are involved in coordinating various physiological functions, including digestion, immune response, blood coagulation and reproduction[12]. A cardiac serine protease known as corin, which has substrate specificity similar to trypsin, also helps to regulate blood pressure (via the natriuretic pathway).[13] Knockout mice lacking corin have a hypertensive phenotype.
L838: However, GLE (A-1282567.0) was tested in a biochemical assay for inhibition of a variety of human serine and cysteine proteases, including chymase, elastase, cathepsin B, chymotrypsin types II and VII, kallikrein, and urokinase (see below figure). No inhibition by A-1282567.0 was observed at concentrations up to 200,000 nM, providing a selectivity index of greater than 320,000 for inhibition of HCV GT1a NS3 protease (GT1a NS3 protease IC50 = 0.63 nM, Abbott internal data).
L839: Figure 2 GLE Inhibition of Human Proteases in Biochemical Assays
L840: Inhibition of Human Proteases by A-1282576.0 in Biochemical Assays
L841: ProteaseIC50, nMSelectivity, -Folda



Chymase&gt; 200,000&gt; 320,000

Elastase&gt; 200,000&gt; 320,000

Cathepsin B&gt; 200,000&gt; 320,000

Chymotrypsin Type II&gt; 200,000&gt; 320,000

Chymotrypsin Type VII&gt; 200,000&gt; 320,000

Kallikrein&gt; 200,000&gt; 320,000

Urokinase&gt; 200,000&gt; 320,000
L842: a. Calculated from the ratio of the IC50 value for the human proteases relative to the HCV GT1a protease IC50 = 0.63 nM.
L843: Reviewer Comment: GLE demonstrated a high level of selectivity for HCV NS3 protease over the human host proteases that were tested. However, it should be noted that protease inhibitors in humans are promiscuous. There are 115 annotated human protease inhibitors responsible for regulating the activity of the 612 known human proteases (ratio of 1:5)[12]. If GLE were to have off target effects, they may be expected to occur in one of the pathways described above. Indeed, a large proportion (27%) of subjects experienced at least one
L844: Clinical ReviewLarissa Stabinski, MDAimee Hodowanec, MDNDA 209394Mavyret® (glecaprevir and pibrentasvir)
L845: gastrointestinal AE and several individual subjects experienced blood pressure variations, as discussed above. However, given the high background rate of gastrointestinal symptoms and confounding medical histories of subjects with cardiovascular events, it is difficult to credibly establish a causal link. Additionally, and importantly, there were no related signals in pre-clinical studies.
L846: 8.5. Analysis of Submission-Specific Safety Issues
L847: 8.5.1. Hepatobiliary Events
L848: Prior to NDA submission, FDA asked the Applicant to assemble an independent committee of DILI experts and practicing HCV clinicians, to formally assess the hepatic safety profile of GLE/PIB. The request was based on a small proportion of subjects who experienced on treatment direct and indirect bilirubin elevations in the Phase 2/3 safety database, as presented at the Pre-NDA meeting; and, because of the known safety events observed with earlier generation HCV protease inhibitors which were associated with increases in bilirubin and in some cases, associated with liver decompensation and hepatic death in patients with baseline decompensated cirrhosis. This section presents the results of the Hepatic Panel Evaluation and additional reviewer analyses focusing on assessment of cirrhosis baseline health status of the cirrhotic and non-cirrhotic safety population, liver related safety events and laboratory abnormalities.
L849: Expert Hepatic Panel Evaluation
L850: The Expert Hepatic Panel (EHP) was comprised of five physicians with expertise in hepatic disorders and experience with assessment of hepatic safety with the administration of direct-acting antiviral therapy for HCV. The panel included:
L851: (b) (6)
L852: Clinical ReviewLarissa Stabinski, MDAimee Hodowanec, MDNDA 209394Mavyret® (glecaprevir and pibrentasvir)
L853: (b) (6)
L854: The panel was asked to assess all subjects with post-baseline total bilirubin elevations \geq 2 times the upper limit of normal (ULN). The Applicant also asked the panel to review all cases that met their definition of on-treatment hepatic decompensation.
L855: The panel structured their review as follows:
L856: Review of Cases of direct or mixed hyperbilirubinemia (total bilirubin increases above baseline of \geq 2 \times \text{ULN} with direct/total bilirubin ratio &gt; 0.4), without increases in ALT Grade (N=4 of 2,369 subjects in the safety database including subjects with severe renal impairment)
L857: Review of Cases of Subjects with Increases in ALT grade to Grade 2+ (\text{ALT} \geq 3 \times \text{ULN}) and hyperbilirubinemia (total bilirubin increases above baseline of \geq 2 \times \text{ULN}); or Increases in ALT grade to Grade 3+ (\text{ALT} \geq 5 \times \text{ULN}) (N=5 of 2,369 subjects in the safety database including subjects with severe renal impairment)
L858: Review of Cases of Hepatic Decompensation Events (Ascites, Hepatic Encephalopathy, Spontaneous Bacterial Peritonitis, Esophageal Variceal Bleeding) (N=1 of 2,369 subjects in the safety database including subjects with severe renal impairment, as identified by the Applicant)
L859: Review of Cases of cases of post baseline bilirubin elevation \geq 2 \times \text{ULN}, not meeting the above criteria (N=23), of 2,369 subjects in the safety database including subjects with severe renal impairment)
L860: The EHP concluded that none of the cases reviewed were consistent with a "definite," "highly
L861: Clinical Review
L862: Larissa Stabinski, MD
L863: Aimee Hodowanec, MD
L864: NDA 209394
L865: Mavyret® (glecaprevir and pibrentasvir)
L866: likely" or "probable" event of drug-induced liver injury (DILI), based on the Drug Induced Liver Injury Network [DILIN] likelihood causality scoring.
L867: One case (Subject 206205) was considered to be a "possible" event of DILI (likelihood 25% – 49%) but with a most likely alternative etiology ("most likely related to passage of gallstones"). This subject is a 58 year old male with no relevant medical history other than chronic hepatitis C infection without cirrhosis. All liver chemistries were within normal range throughout the study treatment period. On Day 87, at the End of Treatment Visit, the subject's liver chemistries were elevated. The subject had post-baseline increase from nadir of ALT &gt; 5 – 20 × upper limit of normal and an increase in total bilirubin ≥ 2 × upper limit of normal and direct/total bilirubin ratio &gt; 0.4 on Day 87. His alkaline phosphatase, and GGT were also elevated. On Day 106 (Post-Treatment Day 20), after his liver enzymes had normalized, an ultrasound of the liver and biliary system revealed two gallstones without evidence of biliary dilatation. The panel concluded that the concurrent elevations of alkaline phosphatase and GGT were consistent with an obstructive picture likely related to a transient passage of a stone, noting that as an ultrasound was not performed until after the liver chemistries had normalized, thus it would not be surprising that there was no longer any evidence of biliary tree dilatation. See table and graph representations of laboratory values below.

L874: All remaining cases were considered either "unlikely" events of DILI or events not reflective of liver injury (N/A). The cases were attributed to underlying Gilbert's syndrome and/or transporter effect of the regimen.
L875: Reviewer Comment: Overall the reviewer agrees with the hepatic panel on the assessment of individual cases of bilirubin elevation. None of these subjects reported bilirubin-related adverse events (i.e., jaundice), obvious liver-decompensation related events and none resulted in treatment discontinuations. Most of the reported cases of bilirubin elevation had
L876: Clinical ReviewLarissa Stabinski, MDAimee Hodowanec, MDNDA 209394Mavyret® (glecaprevir and pibrentasvir)
L877: indirect predominance (23 of the 30 subjects with total bilirubin \geq 2 \times ULN had direct/total bilirubin ratio &lt; 0.4). In addition, most of the 23 of the 30 subjects with bilirubin \geq 2 \times ULN had elevated bilirubin with indirect predominance prior to starting treatment, consistent with an underlying Gilbert's syndrome, which is estimated to affect 3-7% of the US population. (See additional analysis below).
L878: As a part of the same information request, the review team requested that if the findings of the EHP were that direct bilirubin accumulation was benign that the applicant should fully describe the mechanism. Below is an abstraction of this explanation:
L879: Under physiologic conditions, unconjugated (indirect) bilirubin is taken up into hepatocytes by transporters of the organic anion-transporting polypeptide (OATP) family (particularly by human OATP1B1 and OATP1B3), followed by conjugation with glucuronic acid by uridine diphosphate glucose-glucuronosyltransferase [UGT] 1A1), and finally excreted into bile via an ATP-dependent transport (mediated by MRP2). It has been reported that the conjugated (direct) bilirubin can also be transported from hepatocytes back to sinusoidal blood via the efflux transporter MRP3 and excreted in urine as a compensatory pathway to biliary excretion. The conjugated bilirubin can also be taken up again into hepatocytes in the sinusoid by OATP1B1 and OATP1B3. Since GLE/PIB inhibits OATP1B1/3 and is a weak inhibitor of UGT1A1, GLE/PIB has the potential to impact bilirubin transport and metabolism, including conjugated bilirubin.
L880: Reviewer Comment: Overall, the reviewer finds that this is a reasonable explanation.
L881: Baseline Liver Disease in Cirrhotic and Non-Cirrhotic Safety Populations
L882: As presented in Section 8.2.2, 288 of 2,265 subjects (12.7%) in the Phase 2/3 safety dataset (excluding those with renal failure), 288/2,265 were cirrhotic. Overall, over 70% of subjects had a baseline fibrosis Stage of F0-F1. Enrollment criteria limited inclusion of cirrhotic subjects to those with compensated cirrhosis or CTP A, usually considered to have CTP scores of 5 or 6. Appropriately, there was only 1 subject with a CTP score of \geq 6 in the Phase 2/3 Safety Dataset, excluding subjects in EXPEDITION-4, and one subject in EXPEDITION-4 (M15-462), the trial in subjects with renal failure. For the cirrhotic population the mean baseline CTP score was 5.1 and mean MELD score was 7.3, indicative of mild liver impairment.
L883: Reviewer Comment: Overall the subjects in this study had mild liver disease, only a small proportion with cirrhosis. GLE/PIB will likely be used in broader population of subjects with more advanced fibrosis and compensated CTP A cirrhotic subjects with higher scores (possibly closer to 6). Therefore, the safety findings in the clinical trials may be limited and there remains a risk that liver related events could be observed if this regimen is given to patients with more advanced disease. GLE/PIB is not indicated in patients with decompensated
L884: Clinical ReviewLarissa Stabinski, MDAimee Hodowanec, MDNDA 209394Mavyret® (glecaprevir and pibrentasvir)
L885: cirrhosis and there are no safety data in this population.
L886: Treatment Emergent Clinical Hepatic Events
L887: Hepatic AEs were identified for the Phase 2/3 Safety Dataset, excluding renal failure using the MedDRA High Level Group Term Hepatic and Hepatobiliary Disorders. The overall occurrence of hepatic AEs was low with a total of 3 subjects reporting events of hepatic steatosis, hepatic pain and jaundice. All events were considered grade 1. The event of Jaundice was considered to be reasonably related to study medication by the Investigator. The subjects with these events are described briefly below.
L888: Subject 205104 is a 54 year old Asian male without cirrhosis with a MELD of 6 at baseline. He experienced only the event of hepatic steatosis at TW16, 4 weeks after completing a 12 week course of study drug. On treatment his AST and ALT improved to the normal range, without other laboratory abnormalities except for a mild transient elevation of bilirubin at TW12.
L889: Subject 7010 51 year old white male with cirrhosis and a CTP 5 score experienced hepatic pain shortly after experiencing grade 4 angina pectoris in TW2 (as described in Section 8.4.2). The subject also experienced the AE of nausea starting on TW 1 throughout the study.
L890: Subject 2924 is a 52 year old Asian male without cirrhosis who developed jaundice on study week 12 and subsequently withdrew consent. He also experienced on treatment nausea, lethargy, discolored sputum, asthma and a melanocytic nevus. At Week 4 he had a slight elevation of ALP, which subsequently improved to baseline. AST/ALT were normal at baseline and remained normal over his treatment course. Bilirubin, which was normal at baseline, was slightly elevated after treatment week 1, fluctuated slightly and peaked at treatment week 16 at 2.4 mg/dL. The subject, who's MELD score was 7 at study initiation, had an increase in MELD to 10 at Week 16 (at the time of discontinuation; which was impacted by the elevated bilirubin). This case was not reviewed by the hepatic panel, as his peak bilirubin was just under 2 times the upper limit of normal.

L898: Hy's Law Cases
L899: Hy's Law refers to the observation made by Dr. Hy Zimmerman that drug induced hepatocellular injury (i.e., aminotransferase elevation) accompanied by jaundice had a mortality rate of 10-50%. Hepatocellular injury sufficient to impair bilirubin excretion has been used by the FDA to identify drugs likely to cause severe liver injury. The definition used by the FDA as an indicator of clinical concern for drug-induced liver injury (DILI) includes: ALT or AST &gt;3x ULN, total bilirubin &gt;2x ULN without an initial increase in alkaline phosphatase, and no other explanations for the increases in liver enzymes (e.g. viral hepatitis, pre-existing or acute liver disease, another drug capable of causing the observed injury).
L900: Due to a number of confounding factors, the appropriate application and interpretation of Hy's Law in the setting of treatment trials for HCV infection, in general, is unknown. Despite the limitations of Hy's Law in the chronic HCV infected population, the Hy's Law laboratory criteria were used by the reviewer for capturing all potential DILI cases in order to conservatively evaluate the relevant subjects in the clinical safety database (i.e., hepatic safety population).
L901: There were 5 subjects in the Phase 2/3 safety dataset, that met post-baseline laboratory criteria of ALT or AST &gt;3x ULN and total bilirubin &gt;2x ULN (subjects 20605, 30105, 191101, 406204, 501203). All subjects except Subject 301501, were reviewed by the EHP. Subjects 406204 and 501203 had baseline liver laboratory elevations that fluctuated slightly, but trended down and normalized over the course of treatment. Subjects 20605, 30105 and 191101 are reviewed briefly below.
L902: Subject 20605 was reviewed by the EHP and considered to be the only possible case of DILI (of the cases they reviewed) according to the panel. This case is discussed in detail above.

L919: Other Significant Liver Related Laboratory Events
L920: Of 2,369 HCV-infected subjects treated with GLE/PIB in the Phase 2 and Phase 3 safety dataset, and including those with baseline renal failure from EXPEDITION-4, 0.4% (9/2,369) experienced increases in bilirubin to Grade 3 (&gt; 3 \times \text{ULN}) and 1.3% (30/2,369) experienced bilirubin increases to \geq 2 \times \text{ULN}. The majority of subjects with bilirubin \geq 2 \times the upper limit of normal experienced indirect bilirubin elevations. Grade 2 and above ALT, AST and ALP post baseline elevations were rare and overall ALT and AST decreased on treatment. In the Phase 2/3 dataset excluding those with renal failure, mean ALT levels at baseline were 33.6 (U/L) decreasing to 14.6 (U/L) at the final treatment visit; mean AST levels decreased from 27.6 (U/L) to 15.9 (U/L) at the final treatment visit.
L921: Hepatocellular Carcinoma
L922: There were 6 de-novo cases of hepatocellular carcinoma experienced by subjects in the Phase 2/3 Safety Dataset. Five cases occurred in subjects with cirrhosis and only 2 subjects were (503502 and 603504) were treatment naïve. Subjects 2703 and 3623 were previously noted to have liver nodules and all except 2 subjects had elevated baseline Alpha-fetoprotein (AFP) levels. All, achieved SVR 12 except for subject 1301 who died, and did not return for his Post Treatment Week 12 Visit; this subject's SVR 12 status is not known.

L926: Subject 603504 died 99 days after completing 12 weeks of treatment and did not return for his Post Treatment Week 12 Visit.
L927: Reviewer Comment: Although HCC has been reported among individuals without or with low levels of fibrosis, the risk of HCC increases with fibrosis stage. Most cases of HCV-related HCC occur among patients with advanced fibrosis and cirrhosis. HCC develops at an annual rate of 1%–4% in HCV infected patients with cirrhosis[14]. Although HCC occurs in subjects with all grades of cirrhosis, CPT B and C cirrhosis are independent risk factors for developing HCC in the cirrhotic population[15]. HCC amongst cirrhotic subjects in the Phase 2/3 Safety Dataset (including those with severe renal disease) is 1.3% (5/308), which is consistent with the expected annual incidence of cirrhosis. However, CTP scores were low amongst the overall cirrhotic population and 4/5 cirrhotic subjects with HCC had low CTP scores and MELD scores. In addition, one subject with F0-F1 cirrhosis was diagnosed with HCC on treatment. The effect of DAA treatment on the development of HCC is unknown, but in this case, successful
L928: Clinical ReviewLarissa Stabinski, MDAimee Hodowanec, MDNDA 209394Mavyret® (glecaprevir and pibrentasvir)
L929: treatment of these subjects did not prevent the development of HCC even in the context of relatively mild cirrhosis or fibrosis.
L930: Safety Update:
L931: Two additional subjects (Subject 325103 from Study M13-590 and Subject 3624 from Study M15-410) each experienced a non-treatment emergent event of HCC. Both cases were de novo occurrences of HCC. Neither of these events was considered related to study drug by the Investigator. The Investigator considered the events more likely to be related to longstanding HCV infection for Subject 325103, or cirrhosis and benign vascular nodules on the liver for Subject 3624.
L932: Subject 325103 is a 55-year-old Asian female, non-user of alcohol, current smoker of cigarettes (0.40 pack/day for 33 years), with relevant history HCV infection without cirrhosis, moderate fatty liver, small liver cyst, liver hemangioma at segment (S)7-S8, liver hemangioma over S8, gallbladder polyp, and palmar erythema, experienced an event of HCC Study Day 180, Post Treatment Day 96.
L933: Subject 3264 is a 58-year-old black female, non-drinker, non-smoker, with relevant medical history of chronic HCV infection with compensated cirrhosis, benign vascular liver nodule , portal hypertension, esophageal varices, and splenomegaly, experienced an event of HCC on Day 242 (Post-Treatment Day 129).
L934: Reviewer Comment: These additional cases do not change the reviewer's assessment as stated above. It is notable, however, that one of these 2 subjects did not have cirrhosis at baseline.
L935: Overall Reviewer Comment (Hepatobiliary): Like other HCV regimens containing protease inhibitors, GLE/PIB will be contraindicated in CTP C cirrhosis based on clinically-significant elevated exposures. It is also not recommended for CTP B cirrhosis because safety and efficacy were not studied in this population. However, the primary review team concludes the available data for hepatobiliary events related events do not support additional liver-related labeling at this time. Liver toxicity and HCC will continue to be monitored in the post-marketing setting.

L951: 8.5.3. Neuropsychiatric Disorders

L957: Subjects with neuropsychiatric SAEs:
L958: Subject 102103 is a 51-year-old white male with relevant medical history of mild depression (2010), chronic back pain, insomnia, and opiate dependency, experienced arterial injury and suicide attempt. On Day 51, the subject (age 52 at time of event) experienced worsened depression with symptoms of low mood, suicidal ideation, increased sleep, and decreased appetite. On Day 82 (Post-Treatment Day 24), the subject experienced arterial injury and attempted suicide with self-laceration of bilateral antecubital fossa/brachial arteries and ingestion of eight zopiclone 7.5 mg tablets. Following psychiatric evaluation, a primary diagnosis of suicidal intent and other diagnosis of opiate misuse were made on Day 88 (Post-Treatment Day 30). The Investigator and Applicant considered the event of suicide attempt as having no reasonable possibility of being related to study drug and more likely related to temporal implausibility as the onset of the events was more than 3 weeks after the last dose of study drug.
L959: Clinical ReviewLarissa Stabinski, MDAimee Hodowanec, MDNDA 209394Mavyret® (glecaprevir and pibrentasvir)
L960: Subject 6810 is a 62-year-old white male with relevant medical history of psychotic episode (possibly drug induced), stress reaction episode, intravenous drug use, drug withdrawal, and current methadone user, experienced an event of schizophrenia. From Day 68 to Day 70, the subject did not attend a scheduled pharmacy appointment for methadone collection. On Day 71, the police were contacted to perform a welfare check. The subject was taken to the hospital and exhibited inability to speak coherently, significant weight loss, and poor self-hygiene, and was admitted for the event of schizophrenia. Urine drug screen was positive for cannabinoids. The Investigator and Applicant considered the event of having no reasonable possibility of being related to study drug. They considered the event as being related to cannabinoids and/or methadone withdrawal.
L961: Reviewer Comment: Adverse events in this category were relatively uncommon. Anxiety and depressed mood were slightly higher in the GLE/PIB arm of ENDURANCE-2 regardless of causality. Anxiety and depressed mood were coded as ADRs in approximately 1% of the treatment population of the Phase 2/3 dataset. This was not different than the proportional frequency of events in the placebo arm of ENDURANCE-2 when causality was not applied to the placebo study. However these events occur in the Phase 2/3 dataset at a slightly higher frequency than the placebo study when causality was considered in the placebo study. It is therefore difficult to discern if there is a drug effect; if there is a drug effect it would likely only be seen in a larger study population. However of note, there were only 2 serious adverse events in this category, both in subjects with previous histories of mental health disorders and no additional events above Grade 2. Although a larger proportion of subjects experienced neurologic disorders, these events were also common in the placebo arm and there was no discernable pattern of events. Both seizure events occurred in subjects with a history of either underlying seizure disorder (Epilepsy) or previous stroke. In the latter case, treatment course was only notable for discontinuation of lisinopril at the end of treatment visit because of low blood pressure. The primary review team concludes the available data for neuropsychiatric events related events do not support relevant labeling.
L962: 8.5.6 Gastrointestinal Disorders
L963: The Body System or Organ Class (SOC) with the highest proportion of subjects reporting treatment emergent events in the Phase 2/3 dataset was Gastrointestinal disorders, with 605 subjects (approximately 27%) reporting at least one gastrointestinal AE. Of these, 348 (15% overall) were considered ADRs. Similarly, in ENDURANCE-2, subjects in the gastrointestinal disorders SOC experienced the most events. Fifty-five (27%) of subjects in the GLE/PIB arm and 19 (19%) of subjects in the placebo arm reported events in this class; 30 (15%) subjects in the GLE/PIB arm and 16 (16%) subjects in the placebo arm had GI events that were considered related. In the Phase 2/3 safety dataset, the most common events in this SOC by Higher Level

L1008: 8.7. Specific Safety Studies/Clinical Trials
L1009: Safety in Subjects with Renal Impairment
L1010: The safety of GLE-PIB was assessed in the EXPEDITION-4 Trial. As previously described, this trial enrolled subjects with severe renal impairment, including subjects on hemodialysis. All safety analyses were performed on the safety population, N = 104. For the following analyses, only treatment-emergent AEs are presented. Events were considered treatment emergent if they occurred within 30 days of the last study drug dose. The National Cancer Institute Common Terminology Criteria for Adverse Events (Version 4) was used to grade adverse events and laboratory abnormalities.
L1011: Treatment-emergent AEs are summarized in the table below. There were 350 treatment-emergent AEs in 74 subjects and 42 SAEs in 25 subjects. Approximately half of the AEs were considered drug-related, but no SAEs were considered drug-related. The rates of AEs and SAEs

L1017: Deaths
L1018: At the time the NDA was filed, there had been 1 death in EXPEDITION-4. This death occurred after the subjected completed GLE-PIB therapy and was deemed by the investigator to be unrelated to the study drug. A brief summary of this case is provided below:
L1019: Subject 101602 was a 41 year old man with a medical history that included end-stage renal disease on hemodialysis, cirrhosis, diabetes mellitus, and poorly controlled hypertension. Fourteen days after completing treatment with GLE-PIB, he presented to clinic with impaired speech and difficulty moving his right arm. He was found to have a blood pressure of 249/112 mmHG and a large intraparenchymal hemorrhage. His clinical condition deteriorated rapidly and he expired 17 days after completing GLE-PIB treatment. At last testing (14 days post-treatment), the subject's HCV RNA was undetectable.
L1020: Reviewer comment: This subject had multiple risk factors for intracerebral hemorrhage. Most notably, hypertension is one of the most common causes of intracerebral hemorrhage and this subject was reported to have a blood pressure of 249/112 at the time of the intracerebral bleed. Additionally, there is a two week latency period between the end of therapy and the onset of the fatal AE. Based on the timing of the event and the presence of confounding risk factors, this reviewer agrees with the Investigator's assessment that the intracerebral hemorrhage was unlikely to be directly related to the study drug. Further, review of the narrative and CRF shows that the subject's blood pressure was 170/95 mmHg at baseline and
L1021: Clinical Review
  Larissa Stabinski, MD
  Aimee Hodowanec, MD
  NDA 209394
  Mavyret® (glecaprevir and pibrentasvir)
L1022: 180/90 mmHg at the end of treatment. Given the presence of hypertension at baseline and stable, but elevated blood pressure throughout treatment, this reviewer also believes that there is insufficient evidence to implicate the study drug as an indirect cause of the bleed via hypertension.

L1024: Approximately one quarter of subjects in EXPEDITION-4 experienced SAEs. However, none of these SAEs were considered by the Applicant to be treatment related. Looking at the SOC level, SAEs from the Infections and infestations SOC (7/104, 6.7%) and the Gastrointestinal disorders SOC (6/104, 5.8%) were most common.
L1025: Reviewer comment: All SAEs underwent a causality assessment by both the investigator and the Applicant. There was agreement between the investigator and the Applicant in all cases. Based on review of the available data and narratives, this reviewer agrees with the determination that the reported SAEs are not likely to be related to study drug.

L1030: Cardiovascular SAEs:
L1031: Upon review of SAEs at the PT level, there were a large number of events from several different SOC's that could be pooled together as cardiovascular in nature: cardiac failure congestive, hypertensive cardiomyopathy, mitral valve stenosis, fluid overload, pulmonary edema, hypertension, hypertensive crisis, and superior vena cava occlusion. In total, 6 (5.8%) subjects experienced 12 treatment-emergent cardiovascular SAEs. The majority of cardiovascular SAEs can be categorized as either heart failure events or hypertension events.
L1032: Cardiac failure congestive was the most commonly reported SAE, occurring in 3 (2.9%) subjects. In addition to the three subjects for whom the PT Cardiac failure congestive was reported, a fourth subject was reported to have experienced on-treatment pulmonary edema and volume overload. A brief summary of each potential heart failure SAE is provided below.
L1033: Subject 908605 is a 63 year-old man with a medical history that includes stage 4 chronic kidney disease, hypercholesterolemia, previous tobacco use, and hypertension. On Day 70 of study treatment he experienced a hypertensive crisis with resultant congestive heart failure and a prolonged hospitalization. On Day 74, the study drug was permanently discontinued due to ongoing heart failure

L1035: symptoms. On Day 84 the subject was discharged from the hospital and the events were considered resolved.
L1036: • Subject 306601 is a 57 year-old man with a history of hypertension, diabetes mellitus, stage 5 chronic kidney disease not on dialysis, previous tobacco use, and cardiomyopathy. On Day 12 of treatment he experienced congestive heart failure and required hospitalization. Study drug was continued and he was discharged 2 days later following diuresis.• Subject 103604 is a 57 year-old man with a history of chronic kidney disease on dialysis, ischemic cardiomyopathy, congestive heart failure, diabetes mellitus, hypertension, ongoing tobacco use, and hypercholesterolemia. On Day 45 of treatment he developed symptoms of congestive heart failure and was subsequently hospitalized. His HCV treatment was continued and on treatment Day 48 he was discharged and the cardiac event was considered resolved.• Subject 909605 is a 45 year old woman with a history of hypertension, hyperlipidemia, valvular heart disease, prior tobacco use, and chronic kidney disease on dialysis. She was hospitalized for shortness of breath and pulmonary edema on Day 26 of treatment. This first event resolved with dialysis. Then on Day 63, she presented to the emergency room with shortness of breath and chest pain and was found to have mitral valve stenosis. This was managed medically initially until she ultimately underwent mitral valve replacement on post-treatment Day 47. GLE-PIB was continued throughout these SAEs.
L1037: There were 4 serious hypertensive events reported in 2 subjects:
L1038: • Subject 908605 is described above.• Subject 802602 is a 62 year-old man with a history of hypertension, chronic kidney disease on dialysis, and diabetes mellitus. He experienced worsening of his hypertension on Day 80 of his HCV treatment. The study drug was discontinued on Day 83, with just one day of treatment remaining. On post-treatment Day 3, he was reported as having Hypertensive crisis based on a blood pressure of 200/100 mmHg with associated confusion. He was not reported as experiencing any PTs suggestive of heart failure, though according to the narrative provided, his chest x-ray was notable for “cardiac enlargement and minor pulmonary congestion.” The investigator stated the SAE event of hypertensive crisis was not related to treatment.
L1039: Clinical ReviewLarissa Stabinski, MDAimee Hodowanec, MDNDA 209394Mavyret® (glecaprevir and pibrentasvir)
L1040: In addition to these reported hypertension SAEs, as mentioned previously, subject 101602 who had a history of baseline poorly-controlled hypertension, died of an intracerebral hemorrhage associated with a blood pressure of 249/112 mmHg 14 days after completing his treatment regimen. Please see the discussion above regarding deaths for additional details.
L1041: None of the cardiovascular SAEs were assessed by the investigator or the Applicant to be reasonably likely to be related to GLE/PIB. Additionally, although 2 of the subjects (802602 and 908605) discontinued therapy prematurely due to cardiovascular SAEs, all of the subjects described above achieved SVR12.
L1042: Reviewer comment: This reviewer agrees with the investigators' assessments that it is unlikely that any of the cardiovascular SAEs were related to GLE/PIB use. All of the cases were confounded by medical conditions that placed the subjects at increased risk for cardiovascular events. Additionally, the lack of a comparator arm limits the ability to assess causality.
L1043: Renal SAEs:
L1044: Three subjects (2.9%) experienced one or more of the following serious events pertaining to renal function: chronic kidney disease, end stage renal disease, and renal impairment.
L1045: • Subject 202601 is a 59 year-old man who was dialysis dependent prior to study enrollment. He was reported as experiencing a serious event of chronic kidney disease on Day 115, at which time, he was hospitalized for an uneventful cadaveric renal transplant.• Subject 606604 is a 47 year-old man with a history of hypertension, diabetes, and stage 5 chronic kidney disease. On Day 57, he was noted to have worsening renal function. On Day 74, he was hospitalized with asthenia, diarrhea, vomiting, and hypertension and was started on hemodialysis. Study drug was continued without interruption. Dialysis was ongoing at the time of the last report.• Subject 306601 is a 57 year old man with a history of diabetes, hypertension, cardiomyopathy, and stage 5 chronic kidney disease not on dialysis at the time of enrollment. He experienced congestive heart failure on Day 12 (case described above). On Day 20, he had a second SAE involving hospitalization for worsening renal function and pneumonia. After a prolonged hospitalization, he was discharged
L1046: Clinical ReviewLarissa Stabinski, MDAimee Hodowanec, MDNDA 209394Mavyret® (glecaprevir and pibrentasvir)
L1047: on Day 40, at which time the event of renal impairment was considered resolved. He did not require dialysis and the study drug was not interrupted or discontinued.
L1048: Reviewer comments: Overall, the rate of SAEs in this trial was high compared to the rate of SAEs in other trials included in this application (24% vs 1.0-5.8%, respectively). However, this is anticipated, as subjects with advanced renal disease generally have a high degree of comorbidity. This assumption of increased morbidity among subjects with advanced renal disease is supported by the fact that in the C-SURFER trial of elbasvir and grazoprevir in HCV-infected subjects with advanced chronic kidney disease, SAEs were observed in 23% and 20% of the treatment and placebo arms, respectively.
L1049: Infections and infestations, Gastrointestinal disorders, Injury, poisoning, and procedural complications, and Cardiac disorders were commonly involved System Organ Classes (SOCs) in both the renal and non-renal populations. Notable differences between the types of SAEs observed in the renal and non-renal populations, include an absence of neoplasms in the renal population and a higher rate of SAEs falling under the Cardiac disorders and Vascular disorders SOCs. As described above, there were many SAEs that could be categorized under the label “cardiovascular disorders.” This too is anticipated, as cardiovascular disease is both a risk factor for, and a sequelae of, advanced renal disease. All of the subjects experiencing serious cardiovascular events in this trial had multiple cardiac risk factors and underlying cardiac conditions. Based on the available data from this single trial, it does not appear that the rates or types of SAEs occurring during GLE/PIB treatment differ from the baseline rate of such events among persons with advanced renal disease. Additionally, there were no novel events or any specific pattern of events that suggested a causal association with GLE/PIB treatment in this population.
L1050: Discontinuations due to Adverse Events
L1051: Four subjects (3.9%) discontinued study drug due to adverse events. Two of these subjects discontinued study drug due to cardiovascular events and are discussed above under serious adverse events (Subject 802602 and Subject 908605). None of the cardiovascular events were considered related to study drug. Subject 303603 discontinued treatment due to diarrhea that was assessed to have a reasonable possibility of being related to the study drug. The subject's first episode of diarrhea occurred on Day 2 and led to interruption of study drug. The second episode of diarrhea occurred on Day 27 and led to treatment discontinuation. On Day 32, the diarrhea was assessed as resolved, suggesting a positive de-challenge. Subject 504604 discontinued study drug due to pruritus on Day 61 of treatment. This subject had a history of “itching” and the first event of pruritus was reported on Day 1 of study. According to the narrative, at last follow up, the event was ongoing. Nonetheless, the event was considered to have reasonable possibility of being related to study drug.

L1062: Investigators assessed the causality of each reported AE. Events were classified as having either “reasonable possibility” or “no reasonable possibility” of being related to the study drug. Fifty-one subjects (49%) experienced an AE that was considered reasonably likely to have been related to the study drug. The majority of related AEs were Grade 1 or 2 in severity. There were 5 Grade 3 related AEs reported in 5 subjects (anemia, n = 1; asthenia, n = 1; diarrhea, n = 1; and pruritus, n = 2) and no related Grade 4 AEs were reported. Only two AEs that were assessed to be related to the study drug resulted in treatment discontinuation (diarrhea and pruritus, see above for details). L1066: Laboratory Abnormalities
L1067: The tables below display treatment-emergent, graded laboratory abnormalities for chemistry and hematology parameters in EXPEDITION-4. These analyses present the worst change from baseline per subject. Subjects who had abnormal results for the same laboratory test on more than one occasion are counted only once at the highest toxicity grade. The Common Terminology Criteria for Adverse Events (CTCAE), version 4.0, was used to assign toxicity grades to all laboratory results reported below.

L1075: *The abnormality refers to an elevation below the upper limit of normal
L1076: As shown above, Grade 3 laboratory abnormalities were uncommon overall but were primarily noted in renal function and white blood cell parameters. There was a single subject who experienced a treatment-emergent grade 3 bilirubin abnormality. There were no treatment-emergent ALT, AST, or ALP abnormalities above Grade 1.
L1077: The only Grade 4 laboratory abnormalities involved renal function. Nineteen (18.3%) subjects had Grade 4 elevations in creatinine. Among these subjects, 2 had Grade 2 creatinine elevations at baseline and 17 had Grade 3 elevations at baseline. However, the mean and median creatinine and creatinine clearance were relatively stable throughout treatment (mean creatinine was 6.9 mg/dL and 6.7 mg/dL at baseline and week 84, respectively; and mean creatinine clearance was 0.2 mL/min at both baseline and week 84).
L1078: Reviewer comment: Individual subjects are expected to have fluctuations in creatinine and
L1079: Clinical ReviewLarissa Stabinski, MDAimee Hodowanec, MDNDA 209394Mavyret® (glecaprevir and pibrentasvir)
L1080: creatinine clearance over time. For those subjects who are dialysis dependent, this may reflect the time from last dialysis session. However, the median and mean creatinine and creatinine clearance values across the study population suggest that renal function was relatively stable throughout treatment. Additionally, as previously mentioned, review of clinical renal events revealed that only a single subject had on-treatment progression of renal dysfunction requiring the initiation of dialysis. Therefore, this reviewer does not believe that GLE/PIB led to any clinically significant effect on renal parameters in this population.
L1081: Vital Signs
L1082: Analyses of vital signs were conducted for EXPEDITION-4. Significant fluctuations in blood pressure were observed in some individual subjects. The number of subjects experiencing on-treatment extremes in systolic or diastolic blood pressure is presented below:
L1083: • Systolic blood pressure \leq 90 mmHg AND a decrease of \geq 20 mmHg from baseline, n=7• Systolic blood pressure \geq 180 mmHg AND an increase of \geq 20 mmHg from baseline, n=7• Diastolic blood pressure \leq 50 mmHg AND a decrease of \geq 15 mmHg from baseline, n=4• Diastolic blood pressure \geq 105 mmHg AND an increase of \geq 15 mmHg from baseline, n=6.
L1084: However, the mean systolic and diastolic blood pressure of the safety population overall appeared stable throughout the duration of the study. As noted previously, two subjects experienced treatment-emergent hypertension SAEs and two subjects experienced non-serious treatment-emergent hypertension AEs. Additionally, there were two subjects who experienced non-serious, treatment-emergent hypotension AEs.
L1085: Reviewer Comment: The occurrence of blood pressure- related AEs as well as some extreme fluctuations in blood pressure is not unexpected in this study population. Eighty-seven (84%) of subjects in EXPEDITION-4 had reported a medical history of hypertension, hypertensive cardiomyopathy, hypertrophy of LV due to hypertension, arterial hypertension, or severe hypertension at baseline. The variability in blood pressure observed overtime in some subjects may have been influenced by antihypertensive compliance/noncompliance, salt intake, and time from last dialysis session (for those subjects who are dialysis dependent). The occurrence of both extremely high and low blood pressure recordings also argues against GLE/PIB playing a causal role in blood pressure changes, as it is unlikely that both extremes would result from a single mechanism.
L1086: There were no clinically meaningful trends observed for the other vital sign parameters.
L1087: Electrocardiograms (ECGs)
L1088: There were no apparent trends in ECG findings. Two subjects were noted to have QTc prolongation (476 ms and 479 ms) on their week 12 ECGs. Both of these subjects had normal
L1089: Clinical ReviewLarissa Stabinski, MDAimee Hodowanec, MDNDA 209394Mavyret® (glecaprevir and pibrentasvir)
L1090: screening ECGs. Neither subject experienced any cardiac AEs.
L1091: Adverse Events of Special Interest
L1092: • Hepatotoxicity:
L1093: The sponsor defined potential cases of hepatotoxicity as 1 of the following:
L1094: ○ ALT value &gt; 5 \times ULN and \geq 2 \times baseline, OR○ Post-nadir increase in ALT grade to Grade 2 (ALT value &gt; 3 \times ULN) and a concurrent total bilirubin \geq 2 \times ULN with direct bilirubin:total bilirubin ratio &gt; 0.4.
L1095: No subjects fulfilled these criteria for hepatotoxicity. Additionally, no subjects met Hy's Law. Of note, DAVP requested that the Sponsor have an Expert Hepatic Panel (EHP) assess all cases involving on-treatment bilirubin elevations \geq 2 \times ULN. Two subjects from EXPEDITION-4 had bilirubin elevations \geq 2 \times ULN and were evaluated by the EHP (subject 501607 and subject 606603). In both cases, the total and direct bilirubin levels increased early in the treatment course, remained elevated throughout treatment, and returned to baseline by post-treatment Day 29. Neither case was associated with any increase in aminotransferase levels or clinical signs of acute hepatitis or hepatic decompensation. Neither case necessitated treatment interruption or discontinuation. The EHP concluded that drug induced liver injury (DILI) was unlikely in both cases. The panel attributed the increases in bilirubin to a transporter effect.
L1096: • Reviewer comment: The temporal association between the observed bilirubin changes and the starting and stopping of GLE/PIB could be explained by a transporter effect as concluded by the EHP. The absence of aminotransferase elevation and the ability of both of these subjects to complete treatment, suggests that these cases are unlikely to be representative of DILI.• Hepatic decompensation/Hepatic Failure:There were no events clearly suggestive of hepatic decompensation or hepatic failure (i.e. no reports of hepatic encephalopathy, esophageal variceal bleeding, or spontaneous bacterial peritonitis). Deeper investigation revealed non-specific PTs that could be attributable to hepatic decompensation. This reviewer has grouped these events into those with the potential to represent variceal bleeding and those with the potential to represent hepatic encephalopathy. The events are summarized below.○ Three subjects experienced gastrointestinal bleeding during the treatment
L1097: Clinical ReviewLarissa Stabinski, MDAimee Hodowanec, MDNDA 209394Mavyret® (glecaprevir and pibrentasvir)
L1098: period (PTs: Gastrointestinal hemorrhage (n = 2) and Hematochezia (n = 1)). Two of these three subjects were cirrhotic. One of the cirrhotic subjects had a concurrent diagnosis of large intestine polyp and the other cirrhotic subject had a vascular jejunal lesion, both of which represent plausible etiologies for gastrointestinal bleeding. None of the three subjects had concurrent laboratory changes (bilirubin, INR, or platelets) suggestive of hepatic decompensation. Two of the gastrointestinal hemorrhage events were serious, none led to treatment discontinuation.
L1099: ○ Six subjects experienced an altered level of consciousness during treatment (PTs: Lethargy (n = 2), Somnolence (n = 1), Confusional state (n = 1), Disorientation (n = 1), and Hallucination (n = 1)). Only one of these subjects was cirrhotic. The cirrhotic subject (101603) was noted to have experienced lethargy at the week 1 visit. This subject had an associated increase in bilirubin from 13.0 umol/L at week -1 to 16 umol/L at week 1. His bilirubin was noted to be trending down at the week 3 visit. No other laboratory findings suggested hepatic decompensation. None of these events were serious and none led to treatment discontinuation.● Hepatocellular carcinoma:
L1100: No subjects developed hepatocellular carcinoma.
L1101: Other Safety Events of Interest
L1102: Given the imbalance in psychiatric events between the GLE/PIB and placebo arms in ENDURANCE-2, psychiatric AEs were examined in EXPEDITION-4. Five subjects experienced 6 on-treatment AEs related to depression and anxiety (PTs Anxiety, Depressed mood, and Depression). There were no subjects who experienced Suicide or Suicidal ideation; there were no serious psychiatric AEs, and no treatment discontinuations due to psychiatric AEs. Given the absence of a control arm in EXPEDITION-4, it is not possible to determine if the rate of psychiatric events reported in this study is higher than would be anticipated in this patient population.
L1103: Conclusions:
L1104: Among 104 subjects treated in EXPEDITION-4, 102 (98%) achieved SVR12 and there were no virologic failures. This high SVR12 rate and absence of virologic failures suggests that the presence of renal failure does not impact the efficacy of GLE/PIB. The adverse events observed in this trial were increased in prevalence and severity compared to the adverse events observed
L1105: in other trials in this application. This is likely due to the high baseline rate of morbidity associated with advanced renal disease. Notably, the rate of serious adverse events in EXPEDITION-4 was comparable to that observed in previous HCV trials in subjects with advanced renal disease (i.e. the GZR/EBR trial, C-SURFER). In conclusion, this reviewer believes that GLE/PIB is a safe and effective HCV regimen for subjects with severe chronic renal impairment. It is an important addition to the HCV armamentarium for HCV genotype 2, 3, 5, and 6 infected subjects with severe renal impairment for whom there are currently no other treatment options available.
L1106: 8.8. Additional Safety Explorations
L1107: 8.8.1. Human Carcinogenicity or Tumor Development
L1108: The relatively short duration of GLE/PIB treatment (8-16 weeks) and planned follow-up (generally 24 weeks) in clinical trials limits the assessment for oncologic events in clinical trials. Twenty-six subjects (1%) in the Phase 2/3 safety population, had an event in the Neoplasms Benign, Malignant, and Unspecified SOC, 20 of who experienced treatment emergent events in this SOC. Preferred Terms associated with this SOC are shown below. Overall, there were 8 cases of de-novo HCC, including those in the safety update (see Section 8.5.1).

L1112: Reviewer Comment: Based on the available data from the Phase 2 and 3 trials in the Safety Dataset, there is not sufficient clinical information to assess the carcinogenicity for the GLE/PIB combination regimen. Although a few subjects who experienced HCC and other malignancies did not meet the typical risk profile for cancer, there does not appear to be a clear signal. Some human serine protease inhibitors including human Secretory leukocyte protease inhibitor (SLPI) may have a role in human carcinogenesis[16-18]. However, there was no signal found in animal/toxicology studies for GLE, a serine protease inhibitor that has high specificity for HCV. Surveillance for malignancies, in particular HCC, will continue postmarketing in collaboration with DPV II.
L1113: 8.8.2. Human Reproduction and Pregnancy
L1114: For both GLE and PIB, there were no clinically relevant adverse effects on male or female fertility in mice, embryofetal development in mice or rabbits, or pre/post-natal development in mice. Pregnant and lactating women were excluded from participation for all Phase 2 and Phase 3 trials. However, six pregnancies were reported in the Phase 2 and 3 studies, including
L1115: Clinical ReviewLarissa Stabinski, MDAimee Hodowanec, MDNDA 209394Mavyret® (glecaprevir and pibrentasvir)
L1116: in subjects in arms not included in the Phase 2 and 3 dataset. These included 2 spontaneous abortions, 2 elective abortions, and 2 subjects who are due to deliver in (b) (6)
L1117: Subject 131102 (GLE/PIB 300 mg/120 mg QD for 8 weeks) completed 8 weeks of treatment and had a positive pregnancy test result 28 days after the last dose of study drug. The estimated date of delivery was (b) (6). The safety update report provided follow-up on this subject, who gave birth to a healthy baby on (b) (6)
L1118: Subject 129308 (GLE/PIB 300 mg/120 mg QD for 8 weeks) had a positive urine pregnancy test on Day 29 and discontinued study drug. On Day 70, an abdominal ultrasound showed molar pregnancy and the subject underwent an elective abortion.
L1119: Subject 167303 (GLE/PIB 300 mg/120 mg QD for 12 weeks) had a spontaneous abortion (treatment-emergent SAE of abortion missed) on Day 72 (15 days after last dose of study drug).
L1120: Subject 104205 (GLE/PIB 300 mg/120 mg QD for 12 weeks) became pregnant during the study. The site was informed of the subject's pregnancy on (b) (6) (b) (6) days after the last dose of study drug). The estimated date of delivery is (b) (6).
L1121: Subject 2101 (GLE 200 mg QD + PIB 120 mg QD + RBV × 12 weeks) in Part 1 of the study had positive urine pregnancy tests on Days 85, 113, and 141. The pregnancy resulted in a spontaneous abortion (nontreatment-emergent SAE of abortion spontaneous, considered to be related to RBV).
L1122: Subject 6903 (GLE 300 mg QD + PIB 120 mg QD × 8 weeks) in Part 2 of the study had a positive urine pregnancy test 72 and 101 days after the last dose of study drugs. The subject underwent an elective abortion (nontreatment-emergent SAE of abortion induced, considered not related to study drug).
L1123: 8.8.3. Pediatrics and Assessment of Effects on Growth
L1124: No pediatric data are available for review with this application. However, the Applicant has discussed the size and scope of future pediatric trials with DAVP. In conformance with current regulatory requirements, on July 15, 2016, FDA confirmed agreement with AbbVie Inc.'s Agreed Initial Pediatric Study Plan via IND 127416.
L1125: AbbVie, requested deferral of pediatric studies for the proposed indication in pediatric patients \geq 3 to &lt; 18 years because it is anticipated that the DAA regimen will be ready for approval for use in adults before pediatric studies are complete. A partial waiver was also requested for pediatric patients &lt; 3 years of age on the basis that clinical trials in this age group are impossible or highly impractical because the number of patients requiring treatments is very small.
L1126: 8.8.4. Overdose, Drug Abuse Potential, Withdrawal, and Rebound
L1127: There is no anticipated potential for abuse of GLE or PIB given their poor brain penetration and the fact that their pharmacological targets are not associated with pathways known to be related to reward. The highest doses administered to healthy volunteers is 1200mg once daily for 7 days for GLE and 600 mg once daily for 10 days for PIB. The highest doses administered to healthy volunteers is 1200mg once daily for 7 days for GLE and 600 mg once daily for 10 days for PIB. In Phase 1 studies, among healthy subjects given multiple doses of GLE 700 mg QD or higher, 2 subjects had asymptomatic Grade 3 ALT and 3 subjects had Grade 3 total bilirubin elevations. Other than these rare, asymptomatic ALT and bilirubin elevations, the highest single and multiple doses were generally well-tolerated.
L1128: In cases of overdose, patients should be monitored for clinical or laboratory signs of toxicity and appropriate symptomatic treatment should be administered. GLE and PIB are not significantly removed by hemodialysis.
L1129: 8.9. Safety in the Postmarket Setting
L1130: 8.9.1. Safety Concerns Identified Through Postmarket Experience
L1131: There is no postmarket experience with either GLE or PIB as neither is available on the U.S market or any foreign market.
L1132: Effective February 2017, all DAA labels include a Boxed Warning and a Warning and Precaution regarding the risk of hepatitis B virus (HBV) reactivation, resulting in fulminant hepatitis, hepatic failure and death in HCV/HBV coinfecting patients who received treatment with DAA drugs for chronic HCV infection. This safety information was identified from adverse event reports from the FDA Adverse Event Reporting System (FAERS) and medical literature.
L1133: Clinical ReviewLarissa Stabinski, MDAimee Hodowanec, MDNDA 209394Mavyret® (glecaprevir and pibrentasvir)
L1134: Reviewer Comment: Although no cases were reported in the safety database for GLE/PIB, the same Boxed Warning and Warnings and Precautions language will be included in the label regarding the risk for HBV reactivation in HCV/HBV coinfecting patients who were undergoing or had completed treatment with HCV direct acting antivirals, and who were not receiving HBV antiviral therapy.
L1135: 8.9.2. Expectations on Safety in the Postmarket Setting
L1136: Safety in the postmarket setting can be managed by routine pharmacovigilance activities. While there were few hepatic events, there were also few subjects with cirrhosis (288/2,265) in the Phase 2/3 safety dataset. More serious hepatic events, such as hepatic decompensation or hepatic failure, may occur in the postmarket setting, as has occurred with other approved DAA combinations. Furthermore, when a larger number of patients are exposed to GLE/PIB in the postmarket setting, some are likely to experience higher exposures, and additional safety signals may be identified.
L1137: 8.10. Integrated Assessment of Safety
L1138: The safety database for GLE/PIB includes 2,265 subjects without severe renal impairment and 104 subjects with severe renal impairment who received at least one dose of GLE/PIB at the indicated dose and duration and is considered adequate. The trials included small but adequate numbers of subjects with compensated CTP A cirrhosis and subjects with severe renal disease to assess safety in these subpopulations, respectively.
L1139: Headache (9%), fatigue (8%), nausea (6%), and diarrhea(5%) were the most common ADRs and occurred at greater than or equal to 5% in ENDURANCE-2, the 12 week placebo controlled study of subjects without severe renal impairment. All these ADRs except fatigue occurred at higher rates in the GLE/PIB arm of the placebo controlled trial. They were also reported in fairly similar proportion across major clinical trials in which subjects without severe renal impairment received GLE/PIB. Most ADRs were grade 1 or 2 and mild or moderate in severity across major clinical trials in which subjects without severe renal impairment. In subjects without severe renal impairment there only one serious adverse event, transient ischemic attack, considered by the Investigator as reasonably related to the study drug. The proportion of subjects who permanently discontinued treatment due to adverse reactions was 0.3% (7/2,265).
L1140: In subjects with severe renal impairment in EXPEDITION=4, ADRs observed in greater than or equal to 5% of subjects receiving 12 weeks of treatment with GLE/PIB were pruritus (17%), fatigue (12%), nausea (9%), asthenia (7%), and headache (6%). In subjects treated with GLE/PIB who reported an adverse reaction, 55% had adverse reactions of mild severity (Grade 1). No subjects experienced a serious adverse reaction. The proportion of subjects who permanently
L1141: Clinical ReviewLarissa Stabinski, MDAimee Hodowanec, MDNDA 209394Mavyret® (glecaprevir and pibrentasvir)
L1142: discontinued treatment due to adverse reactions was 2%.
L1143: Given that a small proportion of subjects had post-baseline bilirubin elevations (30/2,369 or 1.3%), an expert hepatic panel was convened, and liver safety was thoroughly examined for this review. Most of the reported cases of bilirubin elevation had indirect predominance (23 of the 30 subjects with total bilirubin \geq 2 \times ULN had direct/total bilirubin ratio &lt; 0.4). In addition, most of the 23 of the 30 subjects with bilirubin \geq 2 \times ULN had elevated bilirubin with indirect predominance prior to starting treatment, consistent with an underlying Gilbert's syndrome, which is estimated to affect 3-7% of the US population. None of these subjects reported bilirubin-related adverse events (i.e., jaundice), obvious liver-decompensation related events and none resulted in treatment discontinuations. Bilirubin elevations were consistent with known inhibition of OATP1B1/3 and UGT1A1. Overall, ALT and AST trended down rapidly in the active treatment groups as HCV viral load decreased.
L1144: The EHP panel identified only one possible case of DILI (likelihood 25% – 49%) but with a most likely alternative etiology ("most likely related to passage of gallstones"). Overall the liver safety evaluation was reassuring, but it should be noted that there were only a small proportion of subjects (approximately 13%) with compensated cirrhosis (CTA) and a relatively low mean CTP score of 5.1. In previous HCV PI containing regimens, patients with decompensated (CTP B and C) liver disease had a higher frequency of liver related events. Based on clinically-significant elevated exposures, GLE/PIB will be contraindicated for patients with CTP C liver disease. Because of a lack of efficacy and safety data, GLE/PIB is not recommended for patients with CTP B liver disease.
L1145: The MedDRA SOC with the highest proportion of subjects reporting treatment emergent events in the Phase 2/3 dataset was Gastrointestinal disorders, with 605 subjects (approximately 27%) reporting at least one gastrointestinal AE of any severity; with similar proportions seen in the GLE/PIB arm of the placebo controlled study. Of these, 348 (15% overall) were considered ADRs. Nausea, diarrhea, dyspepsia, constipation, abdominal pain, abdominal pain upper, abdominal distension and flatulence were all ADRs seen at a frequency of greater than 1%; nausea and diarrhea were seen at the highest frequencies. Overall there were 5 SAEs and 7 Grade 3 or above events in the Gastrointestinal disorders SOC within Phase 2/3 dataset excluding those with severe renal impairment. No serious events were considered causal; one grade 3 event of "Abdominal pain upper" was considered causal. However, most gastrointestinal were mild or moderate events (grade 1 or 2), and were seen in the context of a high background rate of gastrointestinal symptoms in the study population. These ADRs appear uncomfortable but overall manageable for the durations of treatment indicated, and generally, did not impact the ability to complete the duration of treatment.
L1146: Overall approximately 84 (3.7%) of subjects experienced either an increased systolic or diastolic
L1147: Clinical ReviewLarissa Stabinski, MDAimee Hodowanec, MDNDA 209394Mavyret® (glecaprevir and pibrentasvir)
L1148: blood pressure readings and/or experienced an adverse event of hypertension. In addition, 57 (2.5%) subjects experienced either decreased systolic or diastolic blood pressure readings and/or experienced an adverse event of hypotension or orthostatic hypotension. This could suggest a possible blood pressure signal in both directions. However, the majority of the cases, especially amongst those with increased blood pressure had confounding medical histories and trends were difficult to follow given appropriate medical interventions (i.e. titrating or initiating antihypertensives) for blood pressure control. It is also possible that these values reflect natural variation and/or an unmasking of a chronic disease (particularly hypertension) in the context of the additional clinical scrutiny subjects receive in a clinical trial.
L1149: Given that several cases of hypotension occurred in subjects with baseline hypertension on anti-hypertensives with no other explanation for low blood pressure, some of the hypotension may be explained by drug-drug interactions, although a specific interaction has not been identified by the review team to date. Similarly, for those with increased blood pressure, the majority of subjects were on concomitant medications at baseline, either for hypertension or other chronic diseases. Drug-drug interactions, hepatic metabolism or hepatic transporters could also play a role in treatment emergent hypertension, in this context. There were no preclinical cardiac or hypertensive signals. At the time of this review the review team is still examining this issue and it is not being considered for labelling. However, given the relatively short indicated duration of treatment, if a signal was detected, monitoring and treating on-treatment blood pressure elevations, would be sufficient practical intervention and something that would be expected to occur even in the absence of specific labelling.
L1150: Given the frequency of AEs in these categories, Rash and Skin Conditions and Neuropsychiatric Disorders were also closely evaluated in this review. However, no additional major safety issues or concerns specifically related to GLE or PIB were identified in these reviews.
L1151: 9 Advisory Committee Meeting and Other External Consultations
L1152: No Advisory Committee Meeting was held and no external consultations were place by DAVP.

L1181: 5. Warnings and Precautions
L1182: For consistency with labelling of other DAAs, the same language as other DAA labels regarding the Risk of HBV reactivation was added to this section.
L1183: 6. Adverse Reactions: Clinical Trials Experience
L1184: Section 6 had significant changes and additions of data from the controlled trials ENDURANCE-2 and ENDURANCE-3. The following is the currently proposed FDA wording for Section 6:
L1185: Because clinical trials are conducted under widely varying conditions, adverse reaction rates observed in clinical trials of TRADENAME cannot be directly compared to rates in the clinical trials of another drug and may not reflect the rates observed in practice.
L1186: Overall Adverse Reactions in HCV-Infected Adults Without Cirrhosis or With Compensated Cirrhosis (Child-Pugh A)
L1187: The adverse reactions data for TRADENAME in subjects without cirrhosis or with compensated cirrhosis (Child-Pugh A) were derived from Phase 2 and 3 trials which evaluated approximately 2,300 subjects infected with genotype 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, or 6 HCV who received TRADENAME for 8, 12 or 16 weeks [see Clinical Studies (14)].
L1188: The overall proportion of subjects who permanently discontinued treatment due to adverse reactions was 0.1% for subjects who received TRADENAME for 8, 12 or 16 weeks.
L1189: The most common adverse reactions, all grades, observed in greater than or equal to 5% of subjects receiving 8, 12, or 16 weeks of treatment with TRADENAME were headache (13%), fatigue (11%), and nausea (8%). In subjects receiving TRADENAME who experienced adverse reactions, 80% had an adverse reaction of mild severity (Grade 1). One subject experienced a serious adverse reaction.
L1190: Adverse reactions (type and severity) were similar for subjects receiving TRADENAME for 8, 12 or 16 weeks. The type and severity of adverse reactions in subjects with compensated cirrhosis (Child-Pugh A) were comparable to those seen in subjects without cirrhosis.

L1207: 14. Clinical Studies
L1208: Section 14 had a large amount of editing and changes based on review of the efficacy data and changes made to the various treatment durations by genotype and subpopulation. Therefore, this section provides the currently proposed labeling. As labeling negotiations are ongoing, it is expected that some of these presentations may change.

L1214: TN=treatment naïve
